- A change the page sent and got no answer to is not sent again to another station: request ids are a station's own.
- On an iPhone a saved app has storage of its own, apart from Safari's: it is expected to ask to be signed in once more after "Add to Home Screen". Not confirmed without the device.
- Whether a locked iPhone's saved app finds its socket dead or half-open, and how soon its timers run, is not measured here.

### Left for later, each with its brief

- **Web push and badges.** A VAPID key pair for the fleet (a synced document only stations read), a push subscription per saved app kept beside the signed-in browser's entry, each station sending its own notices to Apple's and Google's push services (outbound internet from every station that pushes), Declarative Web Push for iPhone. Needs the saved app (built here).
- **A Face ID gate** (WebAuthn, platform authenticator, on opening the saved app): needs the HTTPS name as its relying-party id, so one registration per home station.
- **Redirecting HTTP to HTTPS by default**: after a month of the certificates renewing by themselves on every platform.
- **Station-to-station calls over TLS.**
- **`TS_PERMIT_CERT_UID`** as a narrower right than operator, set at the machine.

## The conversation view (15 Nov 2026, the owner's request)

"I like the Claude mobile app, simple, lets you focus on working with an agent, lets you talk to it. This is a single pane for focus ... I still can see a need though if you need to get back to terminal arrow etc ... make the functionality the same as Claude code but also keep the best of spandeck." Then: "execute ... the mobile plan". The plan's decisions were taken as it recommends; each is the owner's to overrule, and the ones that matter are marked **(default)**.

**Built: the station side (phase M1) and the view (phase M2).** A station can turn an agent pane's session into the items a conversation view shows, serve them a page at a time, send new ones as they are written, and remember how far the owner has read. The exact calls are in `docs/conversation-api.md`. The view is in the Control Deck (`crates/web/assets/chat.js`, and `md.js`, which draws an agent's markdown); what it does is under "The view", below. Answering a question or approving a plan from the view was built on 16 Nov 2026 (phase M3): see "Answer in place", below.

- **A pane has two faces, on the phone and on the desktop (default):** its terminal, as today, and its conversation: what was said, as readable text, with the tool calls folded away. The terminal stays one tap away and is always the whole truth.
- **Built on the agents' own session files (default).** Claude Code and Codex write each conversation to a file as it happens, and the archive already copies every line of it. The conversation is read from those kept lines, so it is still there after the agent deletes its file, and no shipped migration changed: the index that makes paging cheap is in memory and is rebuilt when asked for.
  - **Both vendors say these files are internal and may change with any release.** So the builder is written to fail in the open. A record it does not know is left out and counted, and every answer carries the count and the kinds ("3 records not understood"), so the view can say that it has stopped understanding and send the owner to the terminal. The raw lines are kept as they were written: a fixed builder reads old sessions again. Fixtures with the shapes of Claude Code 2.1.302 to 2.1.325 and Codex 0.169.0, and made-up words, are in `crates/archive/fixtures/`.
  - **Hooks stay the only thing that decides anything.** The pane's state, its current tool and every approval come from the hooks, as before, and are sent beside the items (`live`). The session file only says what was said.
  - **A reply arrives a block at a time, not letter by letter.** An agent running in a terminal does not write its reply anywhere until a block of it is finished.
- **What an item is.** The owner's message (the words, a count of pictures, and where it came from: typed, queued, another agent); the agent's text, as markdown source; thinking, as a length of time and never its content; a tool call (name, one line of what it did, whether it worked, how long), with its input, output and diff fetched only when its row is opened; a question the agent asked and a plan it proposed, with the answer once given; a sub-agent it started; and notices (compacted, turn ended and how long it took, interrupted, error). A tool call is paired with its result by the id both carry. An item's id is its line in the session and its place in that line, which never changes; the line number is also the cursor ("what came after line N"), so a view that reconnects gets nothing twice and misses nothing.
- **What each agent program gets.** Claude Code: all of the above. Codex: its messages, tool calls with their output, and turn ends; no title, no diff, and whether a call worked is not in its file, so it reads "done". The Codex half was written against real 0.169.0 files, but no live Codex pane has been watched. OpenCode, Antigravity and shells: no conversation; the call says why in a sentence and the view opens the terminal (since 16 Nov 2026 their Chat is made from the screen: "Chat for every pane", below). That a pane was resumed is known from its hooks, not from the file, and is sent in `live`.
- **Who is served, and why the scrubbing Guarantee stands (default).** The Guarantee says other stations and agents see only the scrubbed view of history. The conversation is the raw history, unscrubbed: it has to be, or the owner would read `[redacted]` where the terminal beside it shows the real thing. So it goes to one reader only, **the owner's signed-in browser**, which is neither a station nor an agent, and which is already shown the same words by the pane's terminal (`pane.attach`, `pane.text`).
  - `pane.conversation`, `pane.conversation.detail` and `pane.read` are the owner's: on no agent's list, not on the read-only list, and on no station's list. An agent is refused its own pane's conversation too; it has `recall`, which reads the scrubbed copy, as before.
  - A deck served by another station reaches a pane's conversation as it reaches its terminal: the serving station passes on the owner's request (`rpc.forward`, or for the live stream a connection signed with the fleet key, as for `pane.attach`). **It passes the answer through and keeps none of it**: nothing is written to its archive, its log or its database. What a station can do on another as the owner is not widened by this: it could already attach to the pane.
  - What is new to say plainly: the raw text of a tool's output now crosses the tailnet to the owner's browser as JSON, where before it crossed as terminal bytes. A key an agent printed is in both.
  - Text from a session file is data (rule 6). The station drops terminal escape sequences and control characters and returns text and structure, never markup; the view will insert it as text.
- **The live stream.** While a browser watches a pane's conversation, the station looks at that session's file every 200 ms (the archive's own look is once a second) and sends what is new on the browser's existing connection; when nobody watches, nothing extra runs. Through another station the messages are passed along as a pane's terminal is. The event bus has no replay, and this needs none: the cursor is the resume point.
- **Unread is the fleet's, and is kept at the pane's station (default).** "It's all the same view from spandeck." Each pane has a mark: the line of its session the owner has read to. The mark is kept by the pane's own station (in its key-value table), set through any station's deck as the owner's forwarded request, and every pane in the pane list says `unread`: the agent has said something after the mark. So the dot is the same on every device and whichever station serves the deck. A mark belongs to one session of the pane; it only moves forward; it goes when the pane is closed. Looking at a conversation, marking it read and asking for a tool's detail do not count as the owner being at a keyboard, so a phone showing one conversation is still pushed the notice for another pane.
- **Bounds.** An item's text is cut at 16 KB and a tool's output at 64 KB a page, each with a mark and a way to fetch the rest; a diff at 2,000 lines; a page at 200 items; an index at 100,000 items a session and eight sessions, about 32 bytes an item. Reading is done off the daemon's async work, and the database is asked for a few hundred lines at a time, never held while they are parsed.
- **Cost, measured on the machine it was built on, on a real 51 MB Claude Code session (13,936 lines, 2,961 items):** the first request builds the index in 85 ms; after that, a look with nothing new takes 0.07 ms, and paging through the whole session 200 items at a time takes 85 ms in all. The index was 89 KB. The archive's copy of the file, which exists with or without this, took 0.5 s once.
- **Not built, and not claimed:** a queued message shown before it is sent; a background task's later report tied back to the row that started it; OpenCode's own streaming interface.
- **A station on a version from before this** has none of these calls: asked for a pane's conversation it answers that there is no such method, which the view treats as "no conversation: that station needs an update", and shows the terminal. Its panes have no `unread` and no title in the list. A deck served by an older station is its own older deck, as always.
- **Native app:** unchanged. "In the browser; no native app" stands until the plan's later gate.

### The view (phase M2, 15 Nov 2026)

The owner showed two pictures of the Claude app on a phone: a list of sessions with a dot on the unread ones and "New session", and one conversation with the reply as comfortable text, Copy and Read aloud under it, a "3 running tasks" link and a message box. The view follows them, and keeps what spandeck has that the app has not: the terminal one tap away, the fleet, approvals held by a hook.

- **A pane has two faces, chosen with "Chat | Terminal" on its bar.** Terminal is the pane as it always was. Chat is its conversation. **Chat never attaches to the pane**, so it sets no size and takes control from nobody ("One size per pane"; since 16 Nov 2026 with one exception, on a phone, for a conversation made from the screen of a pane nobody else has open: see "On a phone every pane is read at the phone's text size"): it watches the conversation, and sends what the phone's message box already sent. A shell, an agent spandeck cannot read (OpenCode, Antigravity), an agent that has not said which session it is in, and a pane on a station from before this are their terminal, and the bar says why in the place of the switch ("Terminal only: it runs a shell, not an agent"). A pane that gets a conversation later (its first hook arrives) gets the switch then. (Until 16 Nov 2026. Since then every pane has the switch, and only a pane on a station from before is its terminal: see "Chat for every pane", below.)
- **What the conversation shows.** The owner's messages as bubbles on the right (one from another agent, a schedule or a background task on the left, saying which); the agent's text as markdown at reading size; a run of tool calls folded into one line ("Ran 6 tools · 48 s"), which opens to a row for each call and a row to what the call was given, what came back (cut, with "Show more") and, for an edit, the change as a diff; thinking as "Thought 12 s" and never its content; a question or a plan as the agent wrote it, with the answer once given; turn ended, stopped, compacted and errors as quiet lines; a kind this version does not know as a quiet line with its name. When the station left records out it says how many and offers the terminal. Under a finished reply: Copy, and Read aloud where the browser can speak. The page above is asked for when the top is reached. The log stays where it was read, and follows new items only while the owner is at the end.
- **Above the message box, while the agent works:** "Working · Bash: cargo test · 42 s" (the time by the station's clock, never the browser's against it), with "Peek", which shows the last lines of the terminal; "3 running tasks" when the agent has sub-agents running; and Send becomes Stop while nothing is written.
- **Cards, pinned above the message box.** An approval the hook holds: what it asks, a reason, Allow and Deny (`approval.answer`, as everywhere). **Only what a hook holds gets buttons.** A question or a plan that waits is shown with "Answer in the terminal" (until 16 Nov 2026; since then one that a hook holds has a card of its own: see Answer in place). When the pane needs the owner and there is nothing the view can answer (a picker, a login, a prompt after an approval ran out of time): "Waiting in the terminal: <reason>", the terminal's last lines, the keys 1, 2, 3, Enter, Esc and the arrows (`pane.input`), and "Open terminal". (20 Nov 2026, a fault the owner met: a pane the station says may be stuck waits at no prompt, and has a notice of its own without the terminal's lines and the keys: see "A pane that may be stuck is told so quietly".)
- **The message box.** Sent as the phone's box always sent: one paste, then Enter (`pane.paste`). What is being written is kept for each pane through redraws and between Chat and Terminal. "+" sends a photo, a camera shot or a file (`pane.drop_file`: the station writes it and types its path, so it works for every agent). The microphone dictates where the browser can; where it cannot it says so, and says that it needs the station's HTTPS address when that is the reason. (Since 17 Nov 2026 it can never hold the page: see The microphone.) "/" offers Claude Code's text commands.
- **The phone's list opens on what matters.** In order: **Needs you** (approvals with Allow and Deny, then agents that wait or stopped on an error), **Pinned**, then **Recents**: every agent pane in the fleet, the one that changed state last first, each with its session's title (Claude Code names its sessions) or the pane's name, where it is and what it is doing, and a dot when it has said something not read. "Recent | By station" switches to the list as it was, with every control it had (stations, projects, layouts, Watch, New, Wake, Start, Stop, usage, performance, stopping the fleet): nothing was removed, it is one tap away. "+ New session" stays at the bottom: an agent, a station, a project or folder, "Continue the last conversation" and an optional first message, then the new pane's conversation.
- **Read, and "Next".** A conversation is marked read (`pane.read`) when its newest item is on screen and the page is in front; never by asking for it. "Next ›" on the bar, with a count, goes to what else waits in the fleet: approvals oldest first, then agents waiting for something else, then errors, then agents that finished and are unread.
- **On a computer** the same view is a pane's other face: "Chat" on a terminal's title bar, in a pane at full size and in a layout, and the switch on the conversation's bar leads back.
- **In a layout on a phone the pane shown is the pane itself (16 Nov 2026, the owner's report from a phone).** "Noticed that the window on mobile in a layout is the old style vs a pane not in a layout. The panes in the layout should work like the single panes." Until then the phone's one-pane-at-a-time layout view was the terminal only, with the keys and the old message box, for every pane.
  - **The same code, not a copy.** The pane a layout is showing is drawn by what draws a pane opened by itself: the conversation's bar (back, state, the session's title, agent · station, "Chat | Terminal" or why it is its terminal, "Next ›", the pane's menu), and under it the conversation with its cards, activity line, message box and unread mark, or the terminal with Fit to screen, the usage, Stop this turn, the keys and the message box. The face is the same remembered choice, per device and per pane, wherever the pane is opened. Chat attaches to nothing here either; in the Terminal face the layout view sizes a pane as it did (watching, or fitted and in control).
  - **The layout's own controls are one line under the bar** (45 px): previous, "build › claude · 1 of 2" with the dots, next. A dot is amber or red when that pane needs the owner, and the count turns amber when another pane of the layout does. A tap on the line opens the layout's name and where it is from (or the list of layouts), its map (hidden panes dashed) and "Hide <pane>" / "Show <pane>", **over the pane and not above it**, so opening the map takes no room from the conversation and never resizes a fitted terminal; a tap on a pane in the map, or anywhere outside, closes it. With the keyboard up the line steps aside, as the bars do.
  - **Measured at 390×844** (the deck's own top bar is 104 px in both): before, the layout's header ended at 356 px and the terminal had 339 px (177 px with an approval waiting); now the pane's bar and the line end at 253 px and the conversation's log has 532 px (373 px with an approval's card), against 577 px for the same pane opened by itself.
  - **A swipe left or right still steps**, and is a step only when it is plainly sideways (at least 60 px, twice as far across as up or down, under 0.9 s), did not begin on a message box, a card, the activity line, the open map or a block that scrolls sideways itself (code, a table, a diff), and no text is selected. So reading, scrolling and selecting in a conversation are never a step; over a terminal it is as it was.
  - **Stepping away lets go; stepping back finds it as it was.** The pane stepped away from is no longer watched (`pane.conversation.unwatch`) and its terminal is detached, as before. Its conversation is kept in the page while the layout is on screen: what was read, where it was scrolled to and what was being written. Stepping back watches again from the cursor, so nothing is fetched twice. A terminal's own scroll-back in that window is not kept, as when a pane is hidden. Leaving the layout lets go of all of it but the drafts.
  - **An approval of the pane on screen is its conversation's card, said once**; one of another pane of the layout, or of a pane shown as its terminal, is still a row above the pane, whichever pane is showing.
  - **Not changed:** a hidden pane is skipped by previous, next and a swipe and looked at from the map; panes on other stations; "Next ›" on the bar leads to the pane by itself, not to its place in the layout.
  - **What a test cannot tell:** whether a swipe feels right against iOS's own back-swipe from the screen's edge and against selecting text with a long press; the keyboard and the safe areas with the extra line.
- **An agent's text never becomes HTML (rule 6).** `md.js` parses markdown to a tree and builds elements itself (`createElement`, text nodes); nothing is given to the browser to parse. It makes paragraphs, headings, emphasis, code, lists and task lists, quotes, tables, rules and links, and no other element; the only attributes are `class` and a link's `href`. HTML in the text is the characters typed. A link is http or https or it is not a link, and it opens (new tab, `noopener noreferrer`) only after a sheet has shown where it leads. A picture is never fetched: it is a link with its label. The content policy is unchanged. A test draws text written to attack the page and checks exactly this.
- **The screen reader** is told a finished reply once, in a quiet region of its own; the log itself is silent, so every tool call is not read out. Touch targets are 44 px; text follows the deck's size and theme; holding the microphone has a tap that does the same.
- **One addition at the stations: `pane.titles`**, the titles of this station's panes' sessions. A title is made from what was said, so it is the owner's alone, as the conversation is (on no agent's list, no station's, not the read-only one), and it is not put in `pane.list`, which agents read. It reads only the lines written since it last looked, and does not build a session's index.
- **A pushed notice (ntfy) carries the address of its pane** (`Click`), at the station's own tailnet address, so touching it opens the pane, which on a phone is its conversation. A station with no tailnet address sends none. Not tried against a real ntfy.
- **Decided by default; the owner may overrule each:**
  - The phone's list opens on Recent, not By station (each device remembers which it last chose).
  - An agent's pane opens as Chat on a phone and as Terminal on a computer. (Overruled by the owner on 16 Nov 2026: Chat on both. See "Chat for every pane".)
  - Which face a pane shows is the device's own choice, per pane. It is not saved in a layout, and is not the same on every station.
  - Holding the microphone fills the box and never sends: a misheard instruction to an agent with tools is costly.
  - A question and a plan are answered in the terminal for now. (Until 16 Nov 2026: see Answer in place.)
  - Recents are ordered by when a pane last changed state, not by its last output, so the rows do not move about while agents print.
  - A link asks before it opens, every time.
- **Not built in this phase:** answering a question or a plan in place (built on 16 Nov 2026); choosing the model, the effort or the permission mode from the view; a list of ended conversations; search in the list; holding to talk on an iPhone at the plain address (its browser offers no speech there: phase M4, with HTTPS); web push; the conversation in a phone's one-pane-at-a-time layout view, which is still the terminal (built on 16 Nov 2026: see "In a layout on a phone", above).
- **What a test cannot tell, and wants a look on the owner's phone:** the keyboard and the safe areas; dictation and holding to talk; reading aloud and the silent switch; the camera and photo picker; whether the page comes back after the phone was put away (an open fault of iOS on plain `ws://`).

### Answer in place (phase M3, 16 Nov 2026)

The owner's approval: "execute ... the mobile plan" (15 Nov 2026); this is the plan's phase M3 and its open decision 4, taken as the plan recommends. Until now a question an agent asked (Claude Code's `AskUserQuestion`) and a plan it wanted approved (`ExitPlanMode`) were shown in the conversation and answered in the terminal. Now the owner answers them where the conversation is. `crates/core/src/questions.rs`; the cards are in `chat.js`; the exact calls are in `docs/conversation-api.md`. No migration is added. One line is added to the Guarantees, under Enforcement.

**What it rests on, and what was not run.** Claude Code's hook reference (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md, read 16 Nov 2026), "Tools that require user interaction": "`AskUserQuestion` and `ExitPlanMode` require user interaction"; a `PreToolUse` hook satisfies that when it "returns `permissionDecision: "allow"` together with `updatedInput` that holds the answer, so the tool runs without prompting. Returning `"allow"` alone is not sufficient for these tools. For `AskUserQuestion`, echo back the original `questions` array and add an `answers` object mapping each question's text to the chosen answer"; `answers`: "Multi-select answers join labels with commas"; `updatedInput` "replaces the entire input object, so include unchanged fields"; for a deny, `permissionDecisionReason` is "shown to Claude"; for `ExitPlanMode`, "Claude Code injects the plan content and file path before passing the input to hooks", and its permissions page says a hook that answers it "with `"allow"` and `updatedInput`" lets it run "without a prompt". It was built from that documentation, with the stand-in agent, which asks through the same two hooks and prints the input it is given back. **Then run by hand with the real Claude Code 2.1.325 (Haiku) on 16 Nov 2026**, on a throwaway station, answering through the API: the second entry fires (a question was held within 3 seconds, and the terminal showed "running PreToolUse hooks… 1/2" and no picker); `updatedInput` answers a question; two questions with several choices and "Other" came back as "Apple, Plum, quince", so the ", " is taken; a hold of 20 seconds ran out and the picker appeared in the terminal with nothing answered; letting go put the picker there at once; a plan was held with its text, sent back with a note (Claude Code kept planning and followed the note) and then approved (it left plan mode and went on to an ordinary permission prompt for its Write; which permission mode follows is Claude Code's own default flow, the hook chooses none). **Not yet known: that a hold longer than 600 seconds is honoured** (being run when this was written), and what Esc in the terminal does to a held question. The cards themselves were not driven with the real agent, only the calls under them. Three faults were found by that run and are fixed below ("Found with the real agent").

- **Two hooks for one call.** Hooks that match run side by side (the reference: "its matching hooks run in parallel"), and each handler has a timeout of its own. So Claude Code's entries (version 3) have a second `PreToolUse` entry beside the one every tool call has: matcher `AskUserQuestion|ExitPlanMode` (letters and `|`: a list of exact names), timeout 3600, and a command line that tells the hook it is this one: `SPANDECK_HOOK_DEADLINE=3600 SPANDECK_HOOK_ANSWERS=1 '<spandeck>' hook claude`. The first entry is unchanged: it still tells the station of the call and checks the rules within its 75 seconds, and its refusal wins (the reference: "precedence is `deny` > `defer` > `ask` > `allow`"). The second does one thing: it asks the station to hold the call for the owner, and says nothing in every other case. (Considered and set aside: one entry for every tool with a matcher that leaves these two out. A mistake in that pattern would stop every rule from being checked, and it could not be tried with the real agent here.)
- **The exchange.** The hook sends the call's input to its station (`hook.ingest` with `answers: true`, its own pane's call). When the owner answers, it prints, for a question, `{"hookSpecificOutput": {"hookEventName": "PreToolUse", "permissionDecision": "allow", "updatedInput": {"questions": [...as given...], "answers": {"Which herons count?": "All herons", "Which rivers?": "Avon, Tamar, Dart"}}}}`: the question's text to the label chosen; several labels joined with ", "; what the owner typed under "Other" as typed, alone or after the labels of a question that takes several. For a plan that is approved: allow, with the input given back as it came. For a plan that is sent back: `"permissionDecision": "deny"` with "The owner read this plan in spandeck and did not approve it: keep planning, and do not start the work. The owner's note: ...". In every other case it prints nothing.
- **Nothing is ever answered for the owner.** When the owner does not answer in time, chooses "Answer in the terminal instead", or spandeck is away for longer than a minute, the hook says nothing and the agent shows its own prompt in its terminal, as it always did. A call that is not in a shape the card can show whole (no question text, no options, more than eight questions or sixteen options, two questions with the same text, an input over 256 KB) is not held at all.
- **How long, and why (default).** The station holds a question for **ten minutes**: long enough to see a push on a phone and answer, short enough that someone who sat down at the terminal instead is not kept from the prompt for long. The switch is `question_hold_s` (`[flags]`, or `station.flags`, in force for the next question): seconds; 0 turns holding off, and questions are then the terminal's, as before. The entry's hour is not the hold: it is the most a session can ever be asked to wait, since a running session keeps the entries it was started with, and the owner can raise the hold up to 59 minutes without starting sessions again. The station lets go 5 seconds before the hook's timeout and the hook answers 2 seconds before it, so the station decides and the agent never cancels the hook. The reference gives a command hook 600 seconds when no timeout is named and names no upper limit; **that an hour is honoured has not been run.** If it is not, the hook is cancelled early and the reference says what follows ("a timed-out `command` ... hook doesn't block the tool call ... The call continues through the normal permission flow"): the prompt appears in the terminal, and the card stays until its ten minutes are over (see Not done).
- **While it is held the terminal does not show the prompt**, as with an approval. The card says until when ("Waits here for 9 more minutes, then moves to the terminal", by the station's clock) and has "Answer in the terminal instead", which lets it go at once. The pane's reason says the same for every deck: "asks you a question: answer it in Chat (in the terminal from 14:12 UTC)", and on a computer the terminal's title bar has "Answer in Chat" in the place of "Chat". When it has moved: "its question is in the terminal now (nobody answered it here in time)", or "(you chose to answer it there)", and the card becomes the keys for the terminal and "Answer in the terminal".
- **The same record as an approval, so the same promises.** A held question is a row of the approvals' book, with the kinds `question` and `plan`: found again by the hook's random id after a restart (unit 04b), its ten minutes counted from when it was first asked, an answer given while the hook is away kept for it (`delivered: false`), one nobody came back for expired 15 seconds after a start, cancelled when its pane ends. An answer is applied once: a second answer finds nothing waiting; a second try of the same answer with its request id (unit 04a) is told "done" from the question's own record and is never run again. No third mechanism was made.
- **It is in no list an approval is in.** What an agent asked is part of the owner's conversation. `approval.list` (which the read-only caller has), the snapshot and the fleet's "Approvals" never show it, and `approval.answer` refuses it. It is read through the conversation's calls only (`live.questions`), which are the owner's. What is said elsewhere says that something is asked and never what: the pane list has `asks: {kind, until_ms}` and the reason above; the notice reads "<pane> asks you a question" · "Answer it in its conversation. It moves to the terminal at 14:12 UTC."; the ship's log has `question.asked`, `question.answered`, `question.released`, `question.lapsed`, `question.reattached` and `question.collected` with the id and how it stands, never the question or the answer.
- **Who may answer (rule 7).** `question.answer` is the owner's alone: on no agent's list, no station's and not the read-only one; it reaches another station only as the owner's forwarded request, like `approval.answer`. An agent is refused for its own question and for another pane's, and cannot read either.
- **What the owner's browser can put into the agent's call, and no more.** An answer names options by their place in the question, and the station takes the label from the call's own input: the browser cannot send a label. Beside that, only what the owner typed: "Other", 4,000 characters at most, and a note on a plan, the same. The rest of the input goes back exactly as the hook has it. One answer or several is checked against what the question allows, and a question left out is refused.
- **The cards.** A question: its header, the question, each option a large button with what it means; where several may be chosen, tick boxes and Send; "Other…" opens a field. One question with one choice is answered by the tap. Several questions asked together are steps, with Back, and the last step sends them all. A plan: drawn by `md.js` (text, never HTML: rule 6), in a box that opens to the whole plan, with Approve, and Keep planning with an optional note. While it is held, the item in the log is one line, so that nothing is on screen twice; once answered the item shows the answer, or "approved", or "sent back: keep planning". Both are pinned above the message box like an approval, on the phone and on a computer.
- **Needs you, Next and the list.** A pane with a held question needs the owner, like any pane that waits: it is counted in "need you", it is under "Needs you" with "asks you a question" or "asks you to approve its plan", and "Next ›" goes to held questions and plans right after approvals, before what waits in a terminal. A notice's address is the pane's, which on a phone is its conversation, where the card is.
- **Pause, Stop, and after a restart.** *Paused*: Pause is "no new work"; a question belongs to the turn that is running, so it is held and answered as usual, and answering never ends the Pause. *Stopped*: a Stop that reaches the program ends the hold with no answer (the row is cancelled, as an approval's is) and the pane is held as any stopped pane; a Stop of a program that is not reading leaves the question where it is. *Stopped or started again, and then it asks* (its own turn went on): the question is held, and **answering it from the card releases the pane (default)**, because the answer is the owner's input, as typing it in the terminal would be; "Answer in the terminal instead" releases nothing. A turn that ends, is interrupted, or is followed by a new prompt takes its question with it. To overrule the release: say so, and the answer will leave the marks alone.
- **While spandeck is away.** The hook that holds a question waits for the station as a permission request's does (a minute each time it finds it away), asks again with the same id when its connection ends, and finds its question again; it never refuses and never answers. Away for longer than a minute: it says nothing and the prompt is the terminal's. The other hook of the same call is the one that checks rules, and where a rule is enforced it still refuses the call after its minute, question or not.
- **Found with the real agent (16 Nov 2026), and fixed.**
  - *Claude Code asks permission for its own question.* When it shows the picker (after a hold ran out or was let go, and in every session that is not held) it also fires its permission hook for `AskUserQuestion` or `ExitPlanMode`. spandeck held that as an ordinary approval: listed with Allow and Deny, although allowing answers nothing, and still listed after the owner had answered in the terminal. Now a permission request for those two tools is never held: the hook says nothing at once and the prompt in the terminal is the only one. The pane says "asks you a question, in the terminal" (or the plan's words), or keeps the reason the station gave when it let the question go, and Claude Code's own "Claude needs your permission" does not replace either.
  - *A permission request that was settled in the terminal stayed listed.* A request still waiting when the tool call it was for has run (the call's own hook says so: same tool, same command or file), or when its turn has ended, been interrupted or been followed by a new prompt, is over: it is cancelled and leaves every list (`approval.resolved`, `cancelled`, "settled in the terminal"). Before, it stayed until its five minutes were over. This holds for every permission request, not only these two tools. A request for another station's route is not touched.
  - *A row of `approval.list` says what it is*: `tool` at the top of the row, beside `summary`.
  - *A second plan was shown with the first plan's text.* Claude Code keeps a plan in a file and writes what the file held into the call's record when the call is made; when it changes the plan in the same step, the record has the plan from before. The hook is given the plan as it is when the owner is asked. So a plan that was held is shown, as an item and in its detail, with the text its hook was given (kept with the held request, found by the call's own id); a plan that was approved without being held is shown with the plan its result carries; any other with what its record holds, and one whose record holds none says so. The plan file itself is never read: it holds only the newest plan.
- **Other agent programs.** Codex: nothing changes; its entries have no second one, the hook cannot answer for it (`answer_questions` is false), and what Codex asks is in its terminal. OpenCode and Antigravity: no conversation and no hook that can hold; the terminal. A shell: nothing to ask.
- **Who gets it, and what the owner must do.** A Claude Code pane started by spandeck on a station with no global install gets the entries at launch: every pane started or restarted after the update. A station where `spandeck integration install claude` was run keeps version 2 until it is run again (`spandeck integration status` and the doctor say "out of date"). A session that is running keeps what it was started with: its questions stay in the terminal, and the view says so.
- **Mixed versions.** `caps` has `question_answer`. A station from before has no `live.questions`, no `asks` and no `question.answer`: its questions are the terminal's, and the view shows "Answer in the terminal". **A Control Deck from before, showing a pane of an updated station, has no card**: the question is held for its ten minutes all the same, the terminal shows no prompt meanwhile, and that deck shows only the pane's reason, with the time. Update the station that serves the deck, or set `question_hold_s = 0` on the pane's station until then. Going back a version: the second entry's hook is then an ordinary tool-call hook that tells the station of the call a second time (it counts twice in the turn's tool calls, nothing else), and a row of the kind `question` or `plan` that was waiting is expired at that daemon's start, or 15 seconds after it, like any approval whose hook does not come back; until then it is listed among the approvals.
- **Decided by default; the owner may overrule each:** ten minutes, and an hour as the most; one tap answers a question that has one choice (the alternative: a tap selects and Send sends); an answer from the card releases a stopped or restarted pane; a held question is not shown among the approvals of the fleet's top bar; the notice does not say what is asked.
- **Tests.** Every change: the owner's choices to the `answers` object, and what is refused; the exchange in Claude Code's dialect, for a question, a plan approved and a plan sent back; how long a question is held; what is shown is cleaned text (`questions.rs`); a held question is in no list of approvals and can be let go (`approvals.rs`); the hook for a question waits and never refuses, for every state of the guard (`guard.rs`); the installed entries (`install.rs`); who may call `question.answer` (`rpc.rs`); and at a phone's width, in the browser scenario that already shows a conversation: the pane under "Needs you", the card, two questions as steps with several choices and "Other", the answer arriving once, a plan drawn as markdown and sent back with a note, with text written to attack the page (`phase5.rs`, about 1.7 s). Merge tier (`crates/cli/tests/questions.rs`, 2 s): a question, a plan approved and sent back, the hold running out, "Answer in the terminal instead", an agent refused for its own and another pane's question and unable to read it, bad answers refused, Pause, Stop and release, a second try by its request id, the agent's own permission request for a question shown in the terminal held nowhere, a permission request closed when its tool call has run, a plan shown as its hook was given it; (`crates/archive`) an approved plan shown as it was approved, with the shapes of the real session; (`away.rs`) a question through a restart in place, through the daemon ended and started under its hook, and answered while its hook is away, each answered once.
- **Not done here, and said so.** Not yet known with the real Claude Code: that an hour, or anything over 600 seconds, is honoured. (Its session file marks a plan a hook refused with `toolDenialKind`, so the view's "sent back" does not rest on spandeck's own words in the result, though it looks for those too.) The text a held plan's hook was given is found among the 200 newest requests of the station; older than that, the item shows what its record holds. A hook that the agent cancels, or whose turn the owner interrupts with Esc in the terminal itself, is not noticed by the station until its ten minutes are over or the next prompt begins: until then the card is still shown, and an answer given to it reaches nobody (a Stop from spandeck does end the hold). Typing into the terminal does not let a held question go; the card's button does. The fleet's top bar counts the pane, and has no card of its own. The note sent with "Keep planning" is not shown on the item afterwards. The permission-mode chip and "Always allow" are not wanted now. Not run on Windows or macOS.

### Chat for every pane (16 Nov 2026, the owner's report from a phone)

"Every window should have the chat and terminal option, looks like it's just on a Claude window. Even in layouts". With two pictures: a Codex pane (dev2, project testProject1, on spark2) with no switch, only "Terminal only: Codex has not said which session it is in yet (nothing has come from its hooks)" and its terminal; and a Claude Code pane with "Chat | Terminal" and its conversation. Until now a shell, OpenCode, Antigravity, and any agent whose hooks had not named its session were their terminal, and the bar said why.

**Decided: every pane, whatever runs in it, has "Chat | Terminal", on a phone and on a computer, alone and in a layout, and Chat shows something useful for each.** It is done in two layers. `crates/core/src/discover.rs` (the first), `crates/core/src/screentalk.rs` and `Screen::mark` in `crates/terminal/src/screen.rs` (the second), `chat.js`; the calls are in `docs/conversation-api.md`. No migration, no new method, nothing new for an agent.

**What the real Codex 0.169.0 does (run on spark2, 16 Nov 2026, by the owner's session):** with its hooks trusted, a new Codex pane reports nothing until the first prompt: no hook has run, no session is named, and there is no file under `~/.codex/sessions` (Codex begins its session with the first message). After the first prompt its hooks name the session and the conversation is there. So the owner's picture is what every Codex pane shows before its first message, and what one whose hooks were never reviewed shows for ever.

- **Layer 1: the agent's own conversation, without waiting for a hook.** For a pane whose Codex has named no session, the station looks among Codex's own session files (`<CODEX_HOME or ~/.codex>/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*.jsonl`) and takes one **only when it can be no other pane's**:
  - it is one of Codex's own conversations (not one it began for a sub-agent), its first line records the pane's working folder, and it was begun at or after the pane's program was started;
  - no other pane is in it (named by that pane's hooks, or the session that pane was started into);
  - no other pane that runs Codex in that folder, and reports nothing either, had already started when the file was begun. Such a file could be either pane's: it is given to neither, and it never takes the place of a session that is this pane's for certain;
  - of several that are left, the newest, when every older one had gone quiet before it began (one program writes one conversation at a time, and begins another with `/new`). Two written to at the same time are two programs: nothing is taken.
  - **Nothing is guessed.** When none is taken the pane says why, in a sentence, and Chat is the screen (layer 2).
  - **A session a hook names always wins**, and takes the place of one that was found. A pane started into a saved conversation (`codex resume <id>`) knows its session: its file is found by that id.
  - Looked for only while the pane's conversation is asked for: twice a second while none is found, every two seconds after (so `/new` is followed within two seconds). Only directory names and each file's first line are read, in the fourteen newest day folders.
  - **What is found is only read.** A message sent from Chat goes to the pane by its id, as always, never to a session. A question, a plan and an approval are answered only where the pane's own hook holds them (`live`), which a found session has no part in. So the worst a wrong session could do is show the wrong text, and the rules above are there so that it is never another pane's.
  - **Known limit, said plainly:** a Codex the owner starts by hand outside spandeck (or inside a shell pane), in the same folder, after the pane started and while the pane's own conversation is idle, looks like that pane's `/new`: its conversation would be shown in the pane until a hook says otherwise. spandeck does not know of programs it did not start. With the two running at the same time, both files are written to and nothing is taken.
  - **And:** a pane that begins a new conversation while another hookless Codex runs in its folder keeps showing its earlier one (the new file could be either's). The terminal has the truth.
- **Layer 2: the screen as a conversation.** A pane with no transcript to read has Chat all the same: **what was sent to it** (the message box, the paste dialog, `pane.send_text`; from the owner on the right, from another agent on the left with its name) **and what it printed after each**, as plain text in the terminal's own layout.
  - **How it is cut into blocks.** Everything printed between one message sent and the next is one block. The place is a tag on the terminal's row above the cursor at the moment the message is sent (a bit of the cell the engine gives no meaning to): it moves with its line into the history and through a resize. When the next message is sent the block is frozen as text, so an old block never changes whatever the terminal does later. The block still being printed is read from the terminal, and only when something more was printed.
  - **A program that draws its screen itself** (a full-screen program; or one that redrew over the tagged row, as Codex, OpenCode and Antigravity do; or a tag that scrolled out of the 5,000 lines of history): there is no log to cut, so the block is **the screen as it is**, labelled "The screen now", and a frozen one "The screen then". A screen that has not changed since it was last frozen is not said twice.
  - **Bounds.** The page shows the last 30 lines of a block, with "Show N earlier lines"; the station serves the last 16 KB of a block and the rest on request; a frozen block keeps its last 256 KB; the block being printed its last 400 lines ("Earlier lines are in the terminal"); 400 items and 2 MB a pane, the oldest going first. Kept in memory: it begins again when the station does, with whatever the terminal still holds as its first block.
  - **The same component.** It is `pane.conversation` with the same shape (`source: "screen"`), drawn by the same code in `chat.js`: the same bar, bubbles, message box, "+", dictation, Send and Stop, and the same cards (an approval, the folder-trust question, "Waiting in the terminal", held after a stop), since those come from the pane's state. A note stays at the top of the log where "Terminal only" used to be said: **"From the screen: Codex has not said which session it is in yet (nothing has come from its hooks), and no session file of Codex's for this folder has appeared since the pane started (Codex begins one with its first message)"**; one line, a tap shows all of it. That is what a new Codex pane says before anything was said to it, with its screen under the note and the message box ready.
  - **The keys** Esc, Tab, the arrows, Enter and ^C are above the message box in this face (they are on the terminal's face already): the program may be at a prompt nothing here knows of. (Until later the same day: they are behind "Keys" in every pane's message box. See "One chat for every pane".)
  - **When the session appears** (Codex's first message begins its file, or its first hook arrives) the conversation becomes the agent's own, at once, and what was being written stays in the box.
  - **Cheap.** Nothing new is asked: the watch that every conversation has (200 ms, at the pane's station, only while watched) reads the terminal again only when its output count has changed, and sends only what changed. A slow or absent station delays only its own pane, as for any conversation.
  - **Text from a screen is data (rule 6):** the station drops control characters, and the page puts it in a `pre` as a text node. (Since later the same day an agent's is also drawn as prose, by `md.js`, which makes no element from what it reads either.)
- **What each kind of pane gets.**
  - *Claude Code:* its own conversation, as before. spandeck gives it its hooks on the command line at launch, so its session is named before anything is said.
  - *Codex:* before its first message, the screen (there is no session yet). After it: its own conversation, named by its hooks when they run, else found among its files; the screen when two hookless Codex panes share a folder.
  - *OpenCode:* the screen. It keeps its sessions in a database, which the archive reads for usage only; there is no reader that turns it into a conversation, and none was invented. (Until later the same day: its own conversation. See "One chat for every pane".)
  - *Antigravity:* the screen. No fixture or parser for its conversations is in the repository, and no record of which folder one belongs to is known (`agents/sessions.rs`). (Until later the same day: its own conversation. See "One chat for every pane".)
  - *A shell, or any other program:* the screen.
- **Which face a pane opens as: the owner's decision (16 Nov 2026).** "Chat default for agents terminal for terminals". So an agent's pane opens as Chat, **on a phone and on a computer**, alone and in a layout: its own conversation when there is one, the screen otherwise, a new session with nothing said yet included. A plain shell opens as its terminal. The switch is there for both, always. A face the owner picked for a pane is remembered as before (per pane and per device) and wins over this.
  - *What it changes:* until now an agent's pane opened as its terminal on a computer ("An agent's pane opens as Chat on a phone and as Terminal on a computer", a default of 15 Nov 2026, overruled by this). A layout of agents on a computer is now conversations until the owner picks Terminal for a pane; a pane shown as Chat holds no size and attaches to nothing, as before.
  - *What is on a terminal's title bar and not on a conversation's* (the model and the context gauge, "Agents") is one click away, under Terminal. "Run a command in it… Run and open the terminal" picks Terminal for that pane, since it promises the terminal.
  - *A pane on a station from before* has no conversation to give and opens as its terminal, as before.
- **Decided by default; the owner may overrule each:**
  - The keys above the message box in the screen's Chat.
  - The first block of a shell is the prompt it was typed at, with the command as the shell echoed it: the block is the screen, nothing is taken out of it.
  - A line typed by spandeck itself to wake an agent that has mail is in the printed block, not a bubble.
- **Fixed on the way (found with the real Codex 0.169.0 on 16 Nov 2026):** the first bubble of a Codex conversation was "# AGENTS.md instructions for <folder>", which Codex writes as a user message and the owner never said. It is left out, like Codex's other notes to itself (`<environment_context>`, `<user_instructions>`). **No file with that line is among the fixtures:** the rule is the beginning of the text.
- **Mixed versions.** No `caps` entry was needed, and `PROTOCOL` is unchanged.
  - *A deck from before on a station of today:* it does not ask for the screen (`screen: true`), so a pane with no transcript answers `available: false` with its reason, as before, and that deck shows "Terminal only". A Codex pane whose session is found has a conversation there too: the answer has the shape it always had.
  - *A deck of today on a station from before (0.4.7 to 0.4.9), or showing its pane through a station of today:* that station does not know `screen` and answers as it always did: the pane is its terminal and the bar says why, as before. Every pane has both faces once its own station is updated.
- **Tests.** Every change: the cut on the screen through scrolling, a resize, a redraw and a full-screen program (`terminal/screen.rs`); which file is taken and which is not (`core/discover.rs`); a block's id from its first line to frozen (`core/screentalk.rs`); Codex's instructions message (`archive/conversation.rs`); and two station scenarios with the stand-in agent writing Codex's real format with no hooks (`cli/tests/chat.rs`, 3.7 s): the session found after the first message, a deck from before answered as before, two panes in one folder never given each other's, a hook naming another session and winning, an agent refused; and a pane with no transcript served in order, its blocks keeping their ids, a caller told only what is new. Merge tier and nightly, in a browser (`chat.rs`, 5.5 s): at a phone's width, a layout of a hookless Codex and a shell, each with the switch, Chat with content, a message from Chat reaching the pane, the draft kept when the session appears, Terminal one tap away, markup printed by the pane staying text; and the same in a layout's cells on a computer.
- **Not done, and said so.** OpenCode's and Antigravity's own conversations (done later the same day: "One chat for every pane"). A Claude Code session found by its file (never needed: its hooks are given at launch). Telling a pane's Codex from one started by hand in the same folder (above). The session of a Codex started by hand in a shell pane. The unread dot of a Codex pane whose session was found is right only once its conversation has been looked at. What was sent to a pane before the station restarted. **Not run with the real programs:** Codex with no hooks (the format is the fixture's, from real 0.169.0 files), `/new` in a real Codex (that the first file goes quiet before the second begins), OpenCode's and Antigravity's screens in Chat. Not run on Windows or macOS.

### One chat for every pane (16 Nov 2026, the owner's report from an iPhone)

"Claude code chat scrolls great, fast and responsive. Claude terminal is choppy still. Antigravity chat just scrolls the entire pane and not the text inside the pane. Also they look different in chat and should be the same. Shouldn't chat be the same code base for every chat ? Again Claude chat is great . Review the look and feel and experience of Claude to make sure we look and feel the same." With five pictures: an Antigravity pane whose Chat was one framed box of terminal text under "From the screen: …" (indented as the terminal drew it, lines cut at the right edge, markdown shown raw, bullets as dots); an OpenCode pane the same, a tall box of mostly empty rows; a Codex pane's Chat, which looked right, with the keyboard up: the deck's top bar pushed off, the pane's bar half under the status area, the message box in the middle of the screen over an empty gap; the same a moment later, the message box at the very top and everything under it blank; and Anthropic's own app as what to measure against. (The terminal's scrolling is another change of the same day, and is not this.)

**Decided: one chat.** Every pane's Chat is the same component, with the look and the behaviour of the Claude Code conversation the owner praises: one log that scrolls, the owner's messages as bubbles, what the agent said as prose drawn by `md.js`, the same marks under it, the same turn marker, the same message box, the same behaviour with the keyboard. **What differs between agents is what the station sends, never how it is drawn.** `crates/archive/src/stores.rs`, `crates/core/src/reflow.rs`, `screentalk.rs`, `discover.rs`; `chat.js`, `app.css`, and the viewport's few lines in `app.js`; the calls are in `docs/conversation-api.md`. No migration, no new method, nothing new for an agent, no `caps` entry.

- **Antigravity and OpenCode have their own conversations now**, so the screen is the exception it was meant to be. Both stores were looked at on the machine this was built on, read-only, for their layout only.
  - *Antigravity (`agy` 1.5.9)* writes a transcript for each conversation as JSON lines, `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain/<conversation id>/.system_generated/logs/transcript_full.jsonl` (and `transcript.jsonl`, the same with long fields cut at about 4 KB). A line is a step: what the owner wrote (wrapped in `<USER_REQUEST>`, with the program's own notes beside it, which are left out), the model's answer and the tools it called, and what each tool brought back. A call has no id: its result is the step after it, one for each call, in order (so in every one of ten real transcripts), and that is how they are tied. There is no record of a turn's end: an answer that calls no tool is the last of its turn (the program's own hooks guide: a turn stops with `NO_TOOL_CALL`), so "Turn ended" is said there, with no duration. Its hooks name the conversation and the transcript, which is the sure tie; with no hook yet, the conversation is found by the folder its summaries database records (`conversation_summaries.db`), under the rule below.
  - *OpenCode (2.3.0)* keeps everything in one SQLite database, which the archive already read for usage. A conversation's messages are rows (`session_message`), rewritten while a message is being made. **A row is taken only once it is settled, and in order**, and becomes one line of the archive, so a reply appears whole when it is finished (as Claude Code's does, a block at a time). An `idle` row is the turn's end, with how long it took. OpenCode has no hooks: spandeck starts it under a session id of its own choosing, and what is found in its database for the pane wins over that id, so `/new` is followed. The database is opened read-only, only when it has changed, never written and never checkpointed.
  - **A pane is tied to a conversation as strictly as a Codex pane is**, by one rule for the three programs (`discover.rs`, `choose`): begun in the pane's folder at or after the pane's program started, in no other pane, not begun after another silent pane of the same program started in that folder, and the only one still being written; **a session a hook names always wins; nothing is guessed, and when none is taken the pane says why** and Chat is the screen. What is found is only read.
  - **These stores hold the owner's conversations.** They are the programs' own files: opened read-only, and what is read goes where a session file's lines go (the station's archive, raw, served to the owner's browser alone; `recall` and other stations see the scrubbed copy, as for any session). **Nothing from a real one is in the repository**: the fixtures and the tests' stores are made-up words in the layouts learned, and the layouts are described by their fields.
  - **Both vendors' stores are internal and may change with any release**, as Claude Code's and Codex's files are. A step or a row of a kind not known is left out and counted, as everywhere.
- **The screen, when there is no transcript, is drawn as the same chat.** For a shell, an unknown program, an agent before its first message, or one whose store cannot be read:
  - *What an agent printed is given as parts to read* (`parts`), and each is drawn as a reply is: **prose** through `md.js`, in the chat's own type, and **rows whose layout is their meaning** (columns, a drawing) in the block a reply's code has, never wrapped, scrolling sideways inside itself. Only what the rows themselves say is done: a row the terminal wrapped at its right edge goes on in the next (the screen model knows which; nothing is guessed from the width); a row of rule characters is the program's frame and is left out, and a row between two bars keeps what is between them; on a screen the program draws itself, the frame the cursor is in is its message box and the few rows under it its status row, left out; the margin every row has is dropped; a bullet the program drew is a list item; and on such a screen a row of a paragraph goes on from the row above it, since the rows are the program's own wrapping (in a log, every line the program ended stays a line). **No word is changed, and what cannot be read as prose stays as it was**: a block with box-drawing characters or columns set apart by blanks; a list of choices keeps every row, the one the cursor is on too.
  - *A shell's block is its rows*, in the same block style, since there the layout is the meaning.
  - *"The screen now"* is the same treatment of the screen as it is: empty rows closed up, no tall empty box. A screen whose status row alone has changed is not said again.
  - *The log is the one scroller.* There is no box of thirty rows inside it to catch the finger: prose is part of the log, and a block scrolls only sideways.
  - *"Turn ended"* is said when the station sees an agent's turn end (working to idle or done, after at least a second) and no transcript says it: the block printed in that turn is frozen and the notice follows.
  - *The note* ("From the screen: …") stays one quiet line. Copy and Read aloud are under these items too.
  - *The keys* (Esc, Tab, the arrows, Enter, Ctrl-C) are no longer above the message box of a screen's Chat: they are behind "Keys" in every pane's message box, out of the way until asked for. A pane that waits in its terminal has its card with keys, as before.
- **The keyboard, and a page that never scrolls.** What the pictures showed is the browser sliding the page up under what is in sight to show the box being typed in, while the app was only made shorter: its top went off the screen and a gap opened under the message box. Now **the app is exactly what is in sight**: as tall as the visual viewport and placed where it is (`visualViewport`'s size and offset, on its `resize` and `scroll`), a column whose last row is the conversation's message box and whose first is the pane's bar. So the message box is directly above the keyboard and the bar at the top wherever the browser slid to. The body is fixed and cannot scroll or be pulled (`overscroll-behavior: none`); only a log scrolls. Nothing is `position: fixed` to follow the keyboard, and nothing scrolls a box into view. **The log keeps its place** when its room changes: at its end it stays at its end, and further up the line at the bottom edge stays there. The safe areas are padding of the app (top and sides) and of the dock (bottom, not with the keyboard up). The same in a pane by itself and in a phone's layout view, since it is the same component.
- **Look and feel, against the app the owner showed.** Nothing of its marks or colours is copied: the chat follows the deck's themes and sizes as before.
  - *The message box is one card*: what is written above, and under it "+" and Keys on the left, the microphone and a round send button on the right (an arrow; a square while it would stop the turn). The same in every pane. Before: a row of four separate controls with "Send" as a word.
  - *Under a reply*: two small marks, copy and read aloud (their words are still there for a screen reader, and as titles). Before: the words "Copy" and "Read aloud".
  - *While it works*: one quiet line with a dot that breathes, and Peek as a link. *The bubble* is a little rounder; *lines* have a little more air (1.55); the last block of a reply has no space under it.
  - *On a phone the pane's bar is one row*: back, state, title, "Chat | Terminal", the menu. (With "Next ›" showing, the switch takes a row of its own, as before.) Before: two rows.
  - **On a phone a conversation has the screen to itself: the deck's top bar is not above it (default).** What that bar holds is in the pane's menu (Approvals, Inbox, Stop all), the way back is the bar's "‹", and "Next ›" still says what waits. Under Terminal, in the list, and on a computer the top bar is where it was. *Measured at 390×844:* above the conversation there were 210 px (the deck's bar 104, the pane's bar 106) and are 53; the log had 577 px and has 682, with a message box of 96 px where it was 44.
  - *On a computer* it is the same component, with the deck's bar and a pane's frame around it.
- **One code path, and what was made one.** There was one `Conv` already for a phone, a phone's layout and a computer's cell. What was still two: the screen's block had its own drawing (a `pre`) and now is a reply's (`md.js`, and a reply's code block); the marks under a reply are made in one place for both; the keys row was the screen's alone and is every pane's; the dock is drawn before the log, so the log goes to its end in the room it will have, for every source. What the view still asks of the source: the note at the top, the words of an empty conversation, and that a screen has no read mark. **Not made one:** the message box of the *Terminal* face on a phone (`app.js`), which is the terminal's and was being changed by other work the same day.
- **Mixed versions.**
  - *A deck from before on a station of today:* an Antigravity or OpenCode pane whose conversation is read answers with a transcript in the shape it always had, and that deck draws it. A screen's block still has `text` as before, which that deck shows in its box; `parts` it does not know; a "turn ended" notice it draws as any notice.
  - *A deck of today on a station from before (0.4.9):* no `parts`: the block is drawn from `text`, as rows, in the code block's style. Antigravity and OpenCode answer from the screen there, with the old reason. No turn's end is said for a screen.
- **Decided by default; the owner may overrule each:** the deck's top bar steps aside for a conversation on a phone (to have it back: one rule in `app.css`); the keys are behind "Keys"; marks and not words under a reply; a paragraph's rows are joined on a screen the program draws (the other way: every row a line, which reads as fragments on a phone); the title in a program's frame is kept as a line of text; Antigravity's "Turn ended" where an answer calls no tool; an OpenCode reply appears when it is finished and not as it is written.
- **Tests.** Every change: the two stores read (`archive/stores.rs`), their items (`archive/conversation.rs`, with a fixture in the layout of `agy` 1.5.9 and rows in the shape of OpenCode 2.3.0), the rows of a screen as parts (`core/reflow.rs`), a turn's end and a screen said once (`core/screentalk.rs`), the wrap flags and the cursor's row (`terminal/screen.rs`), and two station scenarios (`cli/tests/chat.rs`, about 2 s): an OpenCode pane and an Antigravity pane each tied to its own conversation and to no older one, a reply not shown until it is settled, a hook's conversation winning; and a program that draws 120 columns served as prose with nothing of its frame. Merge tier and nightly, in a browser at 390×844 (`chat.rs`, `every_pane_is_one_chat`): a Claude Code-shaped pane, a Codex one, an Antigravity one and one made from a screen have the same classes and the same drawn type for the bubble, the prose, the marks, the message box; the log is the only thing that can scroll and nothing is wider than the phone; with the keyboard up (the viewport shrunk and slid, and the window itself made short) the message box's bottom is the bottom of what is in sight, the bar's top its top, the page has not moved and the log has kept its place; the same in a layout; and a computer's cell is the same component.
- **Not known, and wanting the real programs:** that Antigravity writes its transcript as a turn goes and only ever adds to it (the reader follows a file that grows); what its hooks really send as `transcriptPath`; when its summaries get a conversation's row; that the real OpenCode keeps the session id it is started under, and how its rows look while a tool runs. **Only the owner's iPhone can tell:** that the message box sits on the keyboard with no gap and the bar stays in sight, in Safari and from the home screen; that nothing jumps when the box is touched; the safe areas; that pulling at the top or the bottom of the log moves nothing else; how the log's scrolling feels now.
- **Not done, and said so.** Antigravity's and OpenCode's questions and approvals in place (neither has a hook that holds one). "Continue the last conversation" for Antigravity. A title for either. The Terminal face's own message box. A collapsing top bar that comes back on a scroll up (it would move the log under the finger; the bar is in the menu instead).

### The chat's bar, tasks, copy, notes and the model (17 Nov 2026, the owner's report from an iPhone)

"On spandeck there is an extra row with a checkbox that's not on Claude code chat, so we need on that? On the top right, there is a circle that show usage. Can we do that? Then there is a row that show how many agents are running as well as a detail window pop that lets you see details of the sub agents/ tasks running. And a small copy icon that you can pick that copies each return to the clipboard. Also there is a model picker, can we add that for all chats ? I think they are just cap the model command and effort." With four pictures: Anthropic's own app in a Remote Control session (a ring at the right of its bar; "3 running tasks" and a "Background tasks" sheet; two marks under the last reply; a "Fable 5.2 Medium" chip in the message box and a "Select model" sheet; the keyboard with no row above it), and spandeck's chat of a Claude Code pane with the keyboard up: about 100 px of empty page between the message box and the row iOS puts above its keyboard ("∧ ∨ ✓"). Then: "Also can I clear the while you were away message?" (Claude Code's own recap, six lines at the end of a conversation for ever). And: **"Also make sure this all this is for any agent chat not just Claude"**.

**Decided: all of it is built once, in the one chat (`chat.js`), for every pane; what differs between agents is what the station can honestly say, never which controls there are.** Where an agent reports nothing the control is still there and says so. `crates/agents/src/models.rs` (new), `crates/archive/src/conversation.rs`, `stores.rs`, `crates/core/src/conversation.rs`; `chat.js`, the chat's block of `app.css`, a few lines of `app.js` (the viewport, the pane's menu); the calls are in `docs/conversation-api.md`. No migration; two new methods, both the owner's only (`pane.model`, `pane.notes.clear`); nothing new for an agent; no `caps` entry (an answer says what it holds).

- **The row above the keyboard is iOS's, and a web page cannot remove it.** It is Safari's form assistant (previous field, next field, done). WebKit shows it on an iPhone for every text field, a `textarea`, an `input` and `contenteditable` alike (`WKContentViewInteraction.mm`, `_elementTypeRequiresAccessoryView`: text, text area and content-editable, on a small screen), and nothing a page can set is asked: no attribute, no `inputmode`, `enterkeyhint` or `autocorrect`, no manifest entry. With one field on the page the arrows are greyed and the row stays. A page saved to the home screen is drawn by the same code: no source says it is absent there, and it is taken to be there. **Only a native app removes it** (it replaces the web view's `inputAccessoryView`; Capacitor's `Keyboard.setAccessoryBarVisible`, Cordova's `HideKeyboardFormAccessoryBar`), which is what Anthropic's app is. "In the browser; no native app" stands, so the row stays. Nothing was done that pretends otherwise: no trick with a field that is not a field, which would cost dictation, autocorrect, paste and selection. (Read 16 Nov 2026: WebKit's source, capacitorjs.com/docs/apis/keyboard.)
- **The gap under the message box was ours in part, and is closed as far as the page can.** With the keyboard up the message box now stands on the bottom edge of what is in sight with **nothing of ours under it**: no safe area and no padding (it had 6 px, and the bottom safe area's 34 px whenever the page did not know the keyboard was up).
  - *The page knows of the keyboard more surely.* It was known by what is in sight being under 78 % of the window's height; a browser that makes the window itself shorter with its keyboard was never under that, so the safe area stayed. It is now measured against the tallest the screen has been at that width.
  - *The page fits again once the keyboard has stopped moving* (50, 350 and 800 ms after a field is entered or left). iOS 26 has open faults here: a visual viewport told of early and not again; "Safari on iOS adds bottom offset to viewport when keyboard is open" (WebKit 292603, open); "VisualViewport.offsetTop not reset after keyboard dismissal ... always a 24px difference" (Apple's forum 800125); 300523.
  - *What may remain is iOS's own.* Whether iOS 26 counts the room of its floating row in the visual viewport could not be established from any source. If it does, a band stays between the message box and that row which no page can draw into; it has the page's own colour, so it is not a band of another colour.
  - **"About this screen", in a conversation's "⋯" menu (temporary):** the numbers that settle it, taken again each second with a box to bring the keyboard up: the window's and the visual viewport's sizes and offset, where the app, the bar, the log, the dock and the message box are, the safe areas, and `under_card`: the room between the message box and the bottom of what the browser says is in sight, **which is 0 when the page has done all it can**. A tap copies them and sends them to the station's log (`client.report`; read with `spandeck rpc log.query '{"kind":"client.report"}'`).
- **The ring.** At the right of the conversation's bar, on a phone and on a computer, for every agent: how full the context is, from the pane's `usage` (the agent's own session data, as the terminal's strip shows), amber from 70 % and red from 90 %, said as "Context 62% used". With a count and no known limit the ring is empty and says the count; **with nothing reported it is a dashed ring that says "Context not reported"**: never a made-up share. A shell has none. A tap opens a sheet of what is known and nothing else: the model, the effort, the context and its limit, tokens in and out, the estimated cost, how long since the conversation was last compacted (when that is among the items read), and the way to the pane's whole Usage.
- **"N running tasks", and Background tasks.** A quiet link above the message box while something runs beside the turn; the sheet lists Running, and Finished folded with its count; the same list is in the pane's menu when nothing runs. A row: a mark for its kind, what it was asked or the command, the kind and how long, how it ended. `live.tasks`, on the watch every conversation has.
  - *Where it comes from, per agent, and nothing else:* **Claude Code**: its session file. A sub-agent is its `Agent`/`Task` call; started in the background its result says so (`async_launched`, `agentId`) and a later `<task-notification>` (tied by `tool-use-id`) says how it ended; one that is waited for ends with its result, or with the turn. A command left running is a call whose result has `backgroundTaskId`, ended by its notice. **OpenCode** and **Antigravity**: the conversations the program began itself under this one (`session_v2.parent_id`; `parent_conversation_id`). OpenCode's is at work while its newest message is unfinished; Antigravity's store has no word for that, so one is listed as running while its transcript was written to in the last fifteen seconds, and its row says "written to just now". **Codex**: its file has none; the sub-agents its hooks report (`SubagentStart`/`SubagentStop`, when its hooks run) are rows with their kind and since when. **Every agent**: the panes it started through spandeck (`delegate`), each with "Open its pane".
  - *A task the file still has as running is said to be lost* when the pane's program has ended or was started again since it began.
  - *"View transcript"* opens a sub-agent's own conversation in the same component, to read only: for Claude Code the end (2 MB, 200 items) of the file it keeps beside the session (`<session>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl`), read when asked for and kept nowhere; for OpenCode and Antigravity the child conversation from their stores. The id that names the file is the program's own, taken only from the session file, and only when it is a plain word.
  - **A stop button only where one task can really be stopped: a helper pane** (`pane.stop`, as for any pane). An agent's own sub-agents and commands have none: Claude Code stops one with a tool of its own or a key in its own screen, and no program outside it can. They stop with the turn, and the pane's Stop is where it was.
- **Copy.** Under **every** reply as soon as it is there (it was under a finished one only), under every block made from a screen, on a code block (its own button, as before) and under the owner's own messages. It copies that item's source (the markdown as the agent wrote it), and says "Copied". Read aloud stays under a reply that is whole. At a plain `http://` address a browser has no `navigator.clipboard`: the copy goes through a hidden box and `execCommand('copy')` inside the tap, as before, now also selecting the way iOS needs.
- **The model chip.** In every agent's message box, beside Keys: the model and the effort **as the agent reports them** ("Fable 5.2 · Medium"), or "Model" when it has reported none. The model is the one behind its latest reply (its session data); the effort is what its hooks say (Claude Code's hook payloads carry `effort.level`: it is in the repository's own fixtures of 2.1.323). The sheet lists what the station's adapter knows for that program, with a tick on the one in use, the effort levels, and "Other…" for any name, so a new model never waits for a spandeck release.
  - **Choosing types the program's own command into the pane, as the owner would, and only while the agent is idle** (`pane.model`, which goes through `agents.command`). In the middle of a turn nothing is typed: the choice is kept by the page and sent when the turn ends, while that page stays open, and the chip says "queued". The chip is never changed by the tap: it follows what the agent then reports, and a refusal is said.
  - *What each program takes, and where that was read (16 Nov 2026):* **Claude Code**: `/model <alias|name>` ("to switch immediately", model-config.md) and `/effort <level>` (commands.md); its documented aliases are the list. It also saves a model chosen this way as the default for new sessions: the sheet says so. **Codex 0.169.0**: `/model <model>` and `/model --reasoning=<level>` (its commands page; `supports_inline_args` in its source); for the session only; no model is listed (its catalogue was not read on this machine: "Other…"). **Antigravity**: `/effort <level>`; its `/model` is a picker and `/model <model> <prompt>` runs a prompt, so no model is typed. **OpenCode**: `/models` is a picker and takes no argument.
  - **A picker is never typed into blind.** For a program with only a picker the sheet has one button, which types the command that opens it, shows the pane's Terminal with the keys, and stops there.
  - A model's name is one word of the characters model names are made of; anything else is refused before anything is typed.
