- **Notes fold, and can be cleared.** Claude Code's recap (a `system` record of the subtype `away_summary`: found by its type, not its words), a background task's report, what a command printed, a compaction's summary and a long line the program showed are each **one quiet line** that opens on a tap, with a small "×". Not an error, a turn's end, a question, an approval or a trust card. The recap's "(disable recaps in /config)" is left off the line; opened, the note says where that switch is. **`/config` is a screen of Claude Code's own, so spandeck does not press it for the owner, and there is no "turn these off" in the menu.**
  - **Cleared means hidden in that conversation on every device of the owner's**: the pane's station keeps which (200 a pane, for the session they are in; `pane.notes.clear`, the owner's only). **Nothing is taken out of the agent's transcript or the archive.** "Show cleared notes" and "Bring cleared notes back" are in the pane's menu.
- **Who may (rule 7).** `pane.model` and `pane.notes.clear` are on no agent's list, no station's and not the read-only one, and reach another station only as the owner's forwarded request. A sub-agent's transcript is served by `pane.conversation.detail`, the owner's already.
- **Mixed versions.** *A deck of today on a station from before (0.4.10):* no `live.tasks`, `live.model` or `live.cleared`: the tasks link counts what the hooks report, as before; there is no chip; a note folds, and "×" says that station has no such method. The ring is drawn from the pane list, which that station has. *A deck from before on a station of today:* it ignores what it does not know.
- **Decided by default; the owner may overrule each:** copy under every reply, not only the last (the app shows it under the last); a mark under the owner's own messages; the ring for an agent that reports nothing (dashed, rather than absent); a choice made mid-turn waits in the page, not at the station (the station's holds are for mail, and a held command would be typed with nobody looking); the short list of Claude Code's aliases rather than every model id.
- **Tests.** Every change: a session file's tasks, started, over and interrupted, and a sub-agent's file as items (`archive/conversation.rs`); OpenCode's and Antigravity's child conversations (`archive/stores.rs`); each program's exact command, and a picker never typed into (`agents/models.rs`); who may call (`core/rpc.rs`); and one station scenario (`cli/tests/chat.rs`, 2 s): tasks running then over, a sub-agent's transcript and no other file, the model and effort as reported, the exact command typed when idle and nothing mid-turn, Codex's and OpenCode's own answers, a note cleared and brought back, every agent refused. Merge tier and nightly, in a browser at 390×844 (`chat.rs`): the ring's value and label, and the dashed one; the link, the sheet and "View transcript"; copy of each reply's source at a plain address; the recap folded, opened, cleared across a reload and a second device, and back; the chip, the sheet with its tick, the command sent and the chip following the agent; the message box on the bottom edge with the keyboard up, in a pane and a layout, and "About this screen" saying 0; and `every_pane_is_one_chat` now holds the ring, the chip and the copy marks equal across the four kinds of pane.
- **Not known, and wanting the real programs:** that Claude Code 2.1.3xx still writes a background command as this reader takes it (**no fixture has one**: `backgroundTaskId` is from the earlier reading of real files, and the notice for a command is assumed to be shaped as a sub-agent's); that a sub-agent that was waited for has `agentId` in its result (else it has no "View transcript"); what Claude Code writes when its own tool stops a task; that `/model <name>` typed into each real program applies without a further prompt (Claude Code may ask about its cache); Codex's levels and model names; whether Codex's hooks really send its sub-agents; OpenCode's and Antigravity's children in real stores. **Only the owner's iPhone can tell:** whether a gap remains (read "under_card", the visual viewport's height and the safe areas from "About this screen" with the keyboard up), and whether the row is there from the home screen.
- **Not done, and said so.** Removing iOS's row (not possible in a browser). Usage for Antigravity (no reader for its token counts; none was invented). A model named on a program's status row (not parsed). A stop for one of an agent's own tasks. A recap switched off from the deck. The Terminal face's own message box has no chip.

#### Found with the real Claude Code (17 Nov 2026), and what was changed for it

`pane.model` was run against the real program (a Haiku session, idle, one turn done). Three things were found.

- **It asks before it switches, and nothing noticed.** After `/model sonnet` Claude Code drew "Switch model? / Your next response will be slower and use more tokens / This conversation is cached for the current model. Switching to Sonnet 5.5 means the full history gets re-read. / ❯ 1. Yes, switch to Sonnet 5.5 / 2. No, go back / Enter to confirm · Esc to cancel". The pane read "done"; the next message sent to it was typed into the question, its Enter answered "Yes", and the message was lost.
  - **The screen is read exactly** (`agents/models.rs`, `switch_screen`; `core/modelswitch.rs`): the screen ends with it, word for word from its title to its last line, the same model is named in its sentence and its first choice, and exactly one choice is marked. Anything else is not it.
  - **After spandeck's own command it is answered "Yes", once.** The owner chose the model in the sheet, and the sheet now says what switching costs before anything is typed. So when that exact screen stands within eight seconds of `pane.model` typing its command, with the program's marker on "Yes", seen at two looks half a second apart, and the owner has typed nothing into the pane since: Enter is pressed, once, and it is in the ship's log (`model.switch.confirmed`). The switch `model_switch = false` (`[flags]`) turns that off: the question is then always the owner's.
  - **At any other time nothing is pressed.** The owner typed `/model` in the terminal, the marker is on "No", it came late: the pane needs the owner, "asks whether to switch model to Sonnet 5.5: answer it in Chat or in the terminal", and Chat has a card with "Yes, switch" (Enter, offered only while the marker is on Yes), "No, go back" (Esc) and "Open terminal". A screen that is that question in other words is still said, by a rule of the screen reader ("asks whether to switch model: answer in the terminal"), with the terminal's keys and no button of its own.
  - **A message is never typed into a question of the program's own.** While this question, the folder-trust question or the review of hooks is on a pane's screen, `pane.paste`, `pane.send_text` and a command (`agents.command`, `pane.model`) are refused, saying what is asked; the message stays in the box. Keys still go: they are the answer. The screen is read at the moment of sending, not only twice a second, and for a second and a half after spandeck's own model command. *How it was:* the message box sent whatever the pane was doing. *What is not covered:* a prompt spandeck does not read exactly (a permission prompt shown in the terminal, a sign-in): there a message is still typed, as before; and a message from another agent that the station types itself when the pane goes idle.
- **Both commands write the owner's defaults.** "Set model to `Sonnet 5.5` and saved as your default for new sessions"; "Set effort level to low (saved as your default for new sessions)": every new Claude Code session on that station, in any project. Its documentation says the same of `/model`, and that only its picker chooses for the session alone.
  - **The sheet says so before the tap**, in the adapter's own words for each program (`saves`), with what switching in the middle of a conversation costs. Codex's `/model` is for its session only and has no such step.
  - **"Only this pane: start it again and resume"** is offered beside it where the program documents the flags (Claude Code: `--model`, `--effort`): nothing is typed and no default changes; the pane's program is ended and started again with the flag and its saved conversation (`--resume`), as "Restart" does. The flags are kept on the pane's record, so a later restart or a recovery starts it the same way. Refused in the middle of a turn, and for a pane with no saved conversation yet. What the agent left running in the background stops with its program.
  - **The typed command stays the first button (default).** It is what the owner asked for ("just ... the model command"), it is instant, and it ends nothing; the other way costs a restart. Each says what it does. To have "Only this pane" first, or the typed command not offered for Claude Code at all: say so.
- **The chip waited for the next reply.** What Claude Code prints for its own command is now read from the session file ("Set model to `<name>`…", "Set effort level to `<level>`…") and shown until the agent's next reply names the model itself. **These are the program's words: no fixture or capture shows a field for either**, so if they change, the chip waits for the next reply as before.
- **Tests.** The screen read only when it is exactly that, and the flags (`agents/models.rs`); the words (`archive/conversation.rs`); and in the station scenario and the browser one (`cli/tests/chat.rs`), with the stand-in agent drawing the question as captured: answered once after spandeck's own command; surfaced and never pressed when the owner typed the command; a message, a command and a second model choice refused meanwhile and nothing of them reaching the pane; the pane as it was once it is answered; the sheet's words before the tap; a pane started again on a model. **No test reads or writes a real agent's settings**: every station of a test has a throwaway home, and the stand-in agent is the only program started.
- **Not known:** that the real program takes Enter the moment the question is drawn (it takes no key in the first moment of its folder-trust screen); that `claude --resume <id> --model <name>` starts as expected and asks nothing; whether the question appears for `/effort` or under other wordings; Codex, Antigravity and OpenCode were not run.

#### The chip did not follow a change (17 Nov 2026, the owner's report from a phone, on 0.4.11)

"when I changed from fable to opus, it either didn't work or it didn't update the model name chat". A Claude Code pane with one turn; the chip read "Fable 5.2". The ship's log had both ways used, the typed command and "Only this pane"; the program's own status line read "Opus 5.5". **The switch had worked, and the chip was wrong**: it named the model of the last reply, and nothing told it of the change.

- **Cause 1: "Only this pane" told the chip nothing.** The pane was started again with `--model opus`, and its resumed conversation's last reply was still by the model before. Now the pane's record keeps when, and where in its conversation, it was started again on the owner's choice, and `live.model.chosen` is the flag's value from the moment the pane is back, until a reply written after that names the model.
- **Cause 2: what the program said was lost with its program.** The words "Set model to …" were known only from the session being read and were compared with the archive's search index for "the last reply". They are now kept on the pane's record (through a restart, and when the program begins another session), and compared with the last reply **that names its model**, in the conversation's own order.
- **The rule, written down** (`model_now`; docs/conversation-api.md): the last word in the conversation's own order holds. A reply written after the program's words, or after the pane was started again, is the truth; one written before is not. In one session the order is the lines'; across sessions the records' own times, a reply with no time being taken as the newer. Of the two that are not replies, the later holds.
- **Not established: why `set` was empty after the typed command on that station, before the restart.** Either the restart lost it (cause 2), or that version of Claude Code writes what a command printed in a record this reader does not take: look at `not_understood_kinds` of that pane's conversation.
- **The program's status line is not read (decided).** What it shows is the owner's to configure (a script of their own draws it), so it has no shape an adapter could match; the banner is an advertisement, not a state. Neither is parsed.
- **Why no effort was shown.** The effort comes from the agent's hooks, and a pane's record begins again when its program is started again: after "Only this pane" there is none until the first hook that carries one (a turn's end, a tool call). It is not read from Claude Code's settings: which level holds there depends on the model, its per-model settings, the environment and the flags, and guessing "Medium" would be the kind of made-up word the chip must not say. With `--effort` chosen for the pane, that level is shown at once.
- **The sheet says where things stand**, in a sentence: "Opus 5.5, as Claude Code said when it was set. It has not answered on it yet. Its last reply was by claude-fable-5-2", or "Opus, as you chose for this pane: Claude Code was started again on it and has not answered on it yet". The chip is dashed until the agent has answered on the model. **What came of the last change from that page is said as a toast and kept at the top of the sheet**: sent, queued, or not changed and why (a refusal by the station included, which before was only the page's general error).
- **Tests.** The rule, in both orders and across sessions (`core/conversation.rs`); a reply that names its model and one that does not (`archive/conversation.rs`); in the station scenario: the words holding through a restart, a reply after them taking over, and `chosen` from the moment the pane is back; in the browser: the chip changing after each way with no new turn, the sheet's sentence, and a refused change said in the sheet.
- **Not known:** whether the real Claude Code keeps its session id when resumed (both cases are handled, the second by the records' times); that its replies after `--model opus` name an Opus model.

#### Sub-agents are shown in a drawer of the agent's pane, for every program (20 Nov 2026, the owner's request)

"I suppose having subagent view, could be inside a agent pane that can pop in and out when you want to see what sub agents are working on?", with a mock-up: closed, a chip in the pane's header ("3 sub-agents · 2 working"); open, a drawer in the pane beside the conversation, a row for each sub-agent (how it is, what it is called, how long, what it was asked, "Now: Bash · studio.layout --shot 7", "Reported back"); one opened, its own conversation, "read only", "‹ claude" back, and "You cannot write to a sub-agent. Write to claude to steer it."

**Decided: the "Background tasks" sheet becomes that drawer, in the one chat, for every pane; and every program's sub-agents are rows of the one list (`live.tasks`), each with as much as its program really says.** `crates/core/src/subagents.rs` (new), `crates/core/src/conversation.rs`, `hooks.rs`; `crates/archive/src/stores.rs`, `conversation.rs`; `crates/agents/src/claude.rs`, `event.rs`; `chat.js` and one block of `app.css`; the row is in `docs/conversation-api.md`. No migration, no new method, nothing new for an agent: the rows are part of `pane.conversation`, and a sub-agent's conversation is `pane.conversation.detail {task}`, both the owner's already (rule 7).

- **The chip.** In the header of the pane's Chat on a computer, between the faces and the ring: "3 sub-agents · 2 working", or "· finished" when none works. **It is there only while there is a sub-agent to show, and takes no room otherwise.** On a phone the same words are the link above the message box, where "N running tasks" was (a phone's header has no room for it).
  - *Which sub-agents it counts:* those at work, and those of the turn now that are over. **A finished one leaves with the owner's next message** (the station marks each row `current`); when the turn's are all over and the next turn begins, the chip is gone. What was is one step away: "Sub-agents and background tasks" in the pane's menu opens the drawer with "Earlier · N" folded.
- **The drawer.** In the pane, to the right of the conversation, which stays in sight and usable beside it (240 to 320 px; in a pane narrower than 620 px it lies over the conversation, inside the pane). A row: a dot for how it is (working, done, failed, stopped), what the program calls it, how long, what it was asked, and one more line: "Now: <its latest step>" while it works, "Reported back: <the first line of what it said last>" once it is done. A sub-agent's own sub-agents are under it, indented. Under them: what else runs beside the turn (a command left running, a helper pane, with its stop and "Open its pane" as before), and "Earlier", folded. **Open or closed is remembered for each pane in that browser** (`localStorage`, `spandeck.subs`); remembered open, it comes back by itself the next time the agent has sub-agents.
  - On a phone the same rows are the full-height sheet that was there.
  - **Where a program says less, a row says less: that a sub-agent is running, what kind, and what it was asked if that is known. Never a guess.** A row has no "Now" unless a record of the sub-agent's own was read for it.
- **One opened.** Its own conversation over the pane's, drawn by what draws every conversation: "‹ <pane>" back, its name, "sub-agent of <pane>", how long, "read only", and under it "You cannot write to a sub-agent. Write to <pane> to steer it." **There is no message box in it, and while it is open nothing can be typed into the pane under it** (the conversation and its message box are `inert`). It follows the sub-agent: read again when its row says something new, at most every second and a half. The pane's own header stays above it (the mock-up replaces it; the faces, the ring and the menu are the pane's and stay where they are).
- **What a sub-agent says is data (rule 6).** What it was asked, its step and its report are the agent's and the sub-agent's own words: cut to one line by the station and put on the page as text.
- **What each program gives, and where it was read (20 Nov 2026, on the owner's machine; structure only, no content):**
  - **Claude Code 2.1.329.** For each sub-agent a file of its own beside the session, `<session>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl` (written to as it works), and `agent-<id>.meta.json`: `agentType`, `description`, `toolUseId` (the call that began it), `spawnDepth`, `parentAgentId` (for one a sub-agent began), and `finished`, which is absent while it runs and `true` once it is over (116 files read; 14 nested; 3 running at the time, all without it). **The meta file ties a sub-agent to its call before any result is written**: one that is waited for has its conversation to read while it runs (17 Nov's "not known": closed). One a sub-agent began has no call in the session's file: it is a row from its meta file, under its parent. How one in the background ended is the session file's `<task-notification>`, as before. *Seen:* all of the above. *Not seen:* a nested one that failed (its row says done or stopped: `finished`, or its parent over without it).
  - **Codex (codex-cli 0.170.0).** Each sub-agent is a thread with a file of its own (`sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-<time>-<id>.jsonl`) whose first record names the thread it was begun under (`session_meta`: `parent_thread_id`, `source: {"subagent": …}`, `thread_source`); the parent's file never names it. It is at work between `task_started` and `task_complete` (or `turn_aborted`) at the end of its own file. Its state database (`state_5.sqlite`) has `thread_spawn_edges` and `threads` (`agent_nickname`, `agent_role`, `first_user_message`, `rollout_path`), read for a name and for a child no file looked at is. **Its own reviewer of approvals is such a thread (`thread_source: guardian_review`, `source: {"subagent": {"other": "guardian"}}`) and is not listed.** *Seen:* the eight child files on the machine, all the reviewer's; the two tables, with no row in the first. **Not seen: a sub-agent Codex was asked to start.** That its file has `parent_thread_id` as the reviewer's has, that `agent_role`/`agent_nickname` are where Codex's public source puts them (`source.subagent.thread_spawn`), and that its first message is what it was asked, are taken from that structure and that source.
  - **OpenCode 2.3.0.** A child conversation is a row of `session_v2` with `parent_id`; the row has `agent`, `time_busy`, `time_idle` and `idle_outcome`, and a `job` table names a child the conversation started (`child_session_id`, `title`, `status`, `output`, `error`). At work: `time_busy` later than `time_idle`; failed: `idle_outcome`. Its step is its newest message. *Seen:* the schema, and seven of the owner's own conversations, each idle with `time_idle` at or after `time_busy`. **Not seen: a child conversation, or a row of `job`**: a job's status words are taken by what they contain (fail, cancel), and a job with no child conversation is not listed.
  - **Antigravity (agy 1.5.10).** A child is a row of `conversation_summaries` with `parent_conversation_id`; the row has `status`, `agent_name` and `killed`, and the parent keeps `brain/<parent>/.system_generated/subagents/<child>.json` (`subagentDescriptor.typeName`, `role`). **The mtime guess is replaced by `status`** (it stays only for a store with no such column, and the row says so). The row's second line is the `role` the program gave it, since what it was asked is not kept apart from its system message. *Seen:* three children, all `CASCADE_RUN_STATUS_IDLE`, their three files, each still `SUBAGENT_STATE_ALIVE` (so that field is not read). **Not seen: a child at work or a killed one**: `…_RUNNING`, `…_BUSY` and `…_CANCELING` are the program's own names for them and were in no row.
  - **A sub-agent's hooks are not the pane's (24 Nov 2026).** Antigravity runs `PreInvocation` and `Stop` for a sub-agent too, with the sub-agent's own `conversationId`. The pane took each as a change of conversation: the owner's pane on LLM-Server, with five sub-agents at work, showed one sub-agent's conversation as its own, no sub-agents (they are looked for under the pane's conversation), and would have read "done" at a sub-agent's `Stop`. An event whose conversation the program began under the pane's (its file is in `brain/<parent>/.system_generated/subagents/`, three deep at most) now changes nothing of the pane and carries no mail. *Seen:* the five files, each written before its child's transcript folder. **Not run with a real `agy` after the change**, and what a child's row says while it is at work was still not read.
  - **Every program's hooks** (Claude Code, Codex): `SubagentStart {agent_id, agent_type}` and `SubagentStop {agent_id, agent_type, agent_transcript_path, …}`. **A stop now ends the one it names** (it ended the first of its kind: with two of a kind the wrong row stayed); a payload with no id is counted as before. A sub-agent only the hooks tell of is a row with its kind and since when; one they said is over keeps a row, and the record they named is read only where it is among the program's own files (beside the session for Claude Code, under `sessions` for Codex). **A start or stop with an empty `agent_type` is the program's own helper and is no event**: it is not listed and no longer counted in `subagents`. *Not seen while this was built:* the payloads themselves (they are as the study of the two programs before it gave them; the tests use those shapes).
- **Bounded.** A watched conversation is asked five times a second. The folder of meta files is listed, and a store asked, at most every two seconds; a meta file is read again only when it was written to; the end of a sub-agent's record (256 KB) is read again only when the record has grown, at most once a second, for 24 rows at most; a Codex file's first record is read once; at most 500 of Codex's files are looked at, and only in a day's folder that was added to since the conversation began.
- **`subagents` means what it meant**: the list the hooks keep, non-empty while sub-agents run. Each entry now also has `id` when the hook gave one. The left list, the fleet's rows and the Terminal's strip read it as before.
- **Decided by default; the owner may overrule each:** finished ones leave with the next message (not after a time, and not at once); the pane's own header stays above an opened sub-agent; a phone has the words above the message box, not in its header; "Reported back" shows the first line of what it said, not a count of findings (the mock-up's "2 findings" is the sub-agent's own words when it says so); a nested sub-agent is indented under its parent rather than a tree with lines.
- **Not done, and said so.** The Terminal face has no chip of its own (its strip says "Running N sub-agents of its own", as before; the drawer is the Chat's). No stop for one sub-agent (17 Nov's decision stands). Writing to a sub-agent. A job of OpenCode's that has no conversation. Token or cost per sub-agent in the row (Usage has it).
- **Tests.** Every change: hook payloads with and without ids, two of a kind, the program's own helper, and a record named outside the program's files (`core/subagents.rs`, `agents/claude.rs`); Claude Code's meta files and a row's "now" from the end of a record (`core/subagents.rs`); Codex's threads with its reviewer left out, its state database, OpenCode's child with its `job`, Antigravity's `status` and `killed` (`archive/stores.rs`, in the real tables' own column names); and two station scenarios (`cli/tests/subagents.rs`, 5 s): a Claude-shaped agent with one at work, one failed, one that reported, one waited for and tied by its meta file, one nested, the step following the record, each one's conversation and no other file, and the next turn; a Codex-shaped one with a thread, its reviewer, and two the hooks alone tell of. Merge tier and nightly, in a browser (`subagents.rs`): the chip and no drawer; the drawer beside the conversation with its rows and a "Now" that changes; remembered across a reload; one opened, read only, nothing typed into it or under it, and back; a narrow pane; a phone's sheet and one opened there; markup in what a sub-agent was asked shown as text; the chip gone after the next message, and "Earlier" from the menu.
- **Wanting the real programs:** a Codex session that delegates (does the child's file have `parent_thread_id`; are its role and first message where they are read; does `thread_spawn_edges` get a row); an OpenCode session that starts a sub-agent (does `time_busy` move as read here; what `job.status` says); an Antigravity sub-agent while it runs (`status`) and one that is killed; Claude Code's and Codex's real `SubagentStart`/`SubagentStop` payloads against the ids of their files (for Codex: is `agent_id` the child thread's id).

### No prompt on the terminal is invisible in Chat (17 Nov 2026, the owner's report)

"When I type /model in Claude it gives me a list to pick from, I see that list in terminal but not in chat. I should see this in chat. Sometime an agent might give a multiple choice picker for questions it has, I'm worried that these won't show up in chat like the model command". And: "Can you make any picker in a nice window, something that look good, can a pick be [done] with a tap and not moving arrows? Or both?"

The worry was right. A conversation read from an agent's session file shows nothing of what the program draws on its terminal: its `/model` list, `/config`, `/resume`, `/permissions`, a sign-in. Only what had a card of its own was seen (a question or a plan a hook holds, an approval, the folder-trust question, the review of hooks, "Switch model?"). `crates/agents/src/picker.rs`, `crates/core/src/picker.rs`, `chat.js`; the calls are in `docs/conversation-api.md`. One new method, the owner's only (`pane.pick`); no migration; nothing new for an agent. (21 Nov 2026: a screen that grants and is read whole has its choices in Chat too: see Safety below.)

- **A list is read by its structure, not its words**, so one nobody has captured is still read. The screen ends with a row of key names; above it, rows in one column of which exactly one carries the program's marker (`❯`, `›`), set in from the left edge. A choice may be numbered, have a second column or words under it, or be two rows with an empty row between entries; `✔` is the one in use, `▸` opens another list; "↓ 31 more"; a field to type a filter in (`⌕`); a few rows between the list and the key names; a title above, up to the program's rule. Taken from four real screens of Claude Code 2.1.326, captured on 17 Nov 2026 and kept as fixtures (the session titles of `/resume` replaced with made-up ones): `/model`, `/config`, `/resume`, `/permissions`.
  - **What is not a list is not read as one**: a marker at the very edge is the program's own message box; a list in an agent's reply has no marker and no key names; a list with no key names under it is not read; nor one with the message box under it (it is over). It is said only when read the same at two looks.
- **In Chat it is a window.** A sheet from the bottom on a phone, a dialog on a computer, the same component for every agent. It opens by itself when the list appears and goes when the program's own goes, by whichever side. The title; the program's description as a quiet line; its tabs as a strip; its search field, typed through to the program a letter at a time; each choice a full-width row of at least 52 px, with the marked row highlighted, a tick on the one in use, a setting's value at the right, a chevron on a row that opens another list; "more rows", also brought by scrolling to the end. A line stays above the message box, for the record and to open it again.
- **A tap chooses; the owner presses no arrows** (`pane.pick`). The station reads the screen, sends the arrow keys that take the program's marker to that row, **reads again, and presses the key only when the marker is on the row with the same words and the list is the same list**. A list that changed, or a marker that did not arrive: nothing is confirmed, the window says so and shows the program's screen with the keys, to finish by hand. While a tap is carried out the row says "choosing…" and the rows cannot be tapped. Where several may be ticked, a tap ticks (Space) and "Done" is the program's Enter. **And keys, both:** on a computer the arrows, Home, End, Enter, Tab, Space and, on the window itself, letters and digits go to the program, and the window follows its marker.
  - *Digits are not used to choose* though Claude Code numbers its rows: that a digit selects is not established. The arrows and the read-back are.
- **`/model`: "This session only" is the first way now.** The real list's key names end "s to use this session only": a choice for the session, with no restart and none of the owner's defaults written. In its window a tap on a row presses `s` there; "Set as default" is its Enter, for the marked row; its effort row has ◀ ▶ (its ←/→). In the deck's own model sheet, "For this session only" is the first button for Claude Code (`pane.model {session_only}`: the command is typed, its list read, the row found, `s` pressed; a model that is not a row of the list closes it with Esc and says so), then "Switch, and keep it as the default", then "Only this pane". `/model` typed in the message box opens the deck's sheet in its place.
- **Safety: what grants is shown with the program's own rows and its choices, and only the owner's tap chooses** (changed 21 Nov 2026, at the owner's request). Until then the rule was "what grants is never a friendly row": such a screen was shown only as the program's rows with the keys, in a window, and `pane.pick` refused it. The owner, on a phone, with Claude Code asking "Allow this read outside the working directories?" and four choices: "this multi choice is not showing up in chat and it should". The owner decides; the rule now:
  - A screen whose title or choices speak of trust, allowing, permissions, bypassing, signing in, an API key, approving or denying is still `screen`, not `picker` (a deck from before shows it as it did). So is one with nothing marked (`/permissions`, whose focus begins on its tabs).
  - **One that grants and is read whole** (`class: grants`, `exact`, its choices in one column, exactly one of them marked, none to tick) **is a card in Chat, where question cards go**: the question in full (its title and the rows wrapped under it, as one sentence); **the program's own rows as they are** (the terminal's last rows, read again each second, placed so that what stands over the question, which is what it asks leave for, is in sight: never only the choices); each choice a button with its number and its whole words (the words wrapped under a choice joined to it), the program's marked row shown as marked and said to be chosen only by a tap; the rows the program draws under its choices; Cancel (the program's Esc), "Its keys" and Open terminal. **It is shown once:** the window does not open by itself over this card; "Its keys" opens it, with the program's screen as it is and the keys (for what a tap cannot do: Tab to amend, scrolling its own text).
  - **A tap is `pane.pick` for that row and no other.** The deck sends the row's words, the words under it, and the question as it showed it (`title`, `about`). The station chooses only when it has itself said the pane shows such a screen (so never the folder-trust question, the review of hooks or "Switch model?"), and only while the screen is, word for word, that question with those choices in that order: before the marker is moved, and again before Enter. Enter is the only key (no "this session" key, no Space). A question that is another by then, a row whose words differ, a marker that did not arrive: nothing is confirmed, the card says why and keeps the rows and the buttons, and the keys are one tap away.
  - **Nothing but the owner's own tap chooses a granting row.** `pane.pick` is the owner's alone (no agent, no station for itself, no read-only caller: `rpc.rs`); `pane.pick`'s own handler is the only caller that may choose from such a screen (the model sheet's way through `/model` cannot); nothing is chosen by default, nothing is remembered for a next time, and no automation calls it. The ship's log names the row chosen and whether it was confirmed.
  - **Unchanged:** the folder-trust question, the review of hooks and "Switch model?" keep their own cards and rules and come first. A screen that grants with nothing marked, or whose structure is not read whole, has the program's rows and the keys and no choices to tap.
  - **Mixed versions, and a switch.** A station that can says `grant_pick` in `caps`; the deck draws the buttons only for a pane of a station that says so. A station from before does not, and its `pane.pick` refuses: the deck shows the window with the rows and the keys, as before. `grant_pick` is also a switch of the station's (`station.flags`, on unless set to false): off, the station does not say it and refuses, for an owner who wants the old rule on a station. A deck from before on a station of today shows the window as it did; its `pane.pick` for such a screen, sent without the question, is refused.
  - **Not known, and wanting the real program:** that Claude Code's own question takes the arrows and Enter as read here (its rows are numbered, and digits are still not used: see above); what stood over the question on the owner's screen (the report has the question and what is under it: the fixture is that, and the stand-in draws a path over it). What the program asks leave for is not read by the station, only shown: a question that kept its words and choices while what stands over it changed between the owner's look and the tap is not told apart.
- **A message is never typed into what the program shows.** The refusal of a message or a command while a question of the program's own is up now covers a list and a `screen`, read at the moment of sending.
- **When nothing is recognised.** A pane the screen reader already says waits ("Waiting in the terminal: …") has its terminal's last rows and the keys in Chat, as before, now with Tab and Space. And after a command of the program's own is sent from Chat (a message beginning "/"), the terminal's last rows and the keys are shown for twenty seconds or until something is written to the conversation: what the command drew, if it is no list, is in sight.
- **Mixed versions.** A station from before has no such `prompt`: nothing is shown, as before. A deck from before on a station of today sees the pane need the owner with the new reason, and "Waiting in the terminal" with its keys.
- **Tests.** Every change: the four real screens read as they are, and what is not a list (`agents/picker.rs`); who may call (`core/rpc.rs`). Merge tier (`cli/tests/chat.rs`): a station scenario with the stand-in agent drawing lists (the third row chosen with one call and confirmed once; the session key; the model sheet's way through the list, and a model not in it; a list that changes under the choice not confirmed; several ticked; a long one scrolled; a message refused; an agent refused; and, since 21 Nov 2026, one that grants, drawn as the owner reported it: read whole, the station saying `grant_pick`, refused without the question the deck showed, with another question, with other words under the row, with any key but Enter and for an agent, each leaving its marker where it was; then the third row chosen with one call and confirmed once; the same rows under a question that became another under the tap not confirmed; `/permissions` given no rows), and in a browser at 390×844 and on a computer: the window for `/model`, `/config`, `/resume`, several to tick, a tap choosing, the keys on a computer; and for one that grants, the card with the question in full, what it asks leave for in sight in the program's rows, four buttons of at least 48 px inside the screen, readable in the four themes, no window over it until "Its keys", a tap on the third choice confirmed once, `/permissions` with no buttons, and with the station's switch off the window as before and no buttons. The reported screen itself, as wrapped at 42 columns, is read whole in `agents/picker.rs`, which also holds the stand-in's drawing to it row for row.
- **Not known, and wanting the real programs:** that the real `/model` list takes `s` with the marker moved by arrows as read here, and what it then writes to the session file; that Enter on `/config` only toggles the marked setting; how the lists of Codex, Antigravity and OpenCode are drawn (none is captured: they are read only if they have this structure; Antigravity's marker `>` at the edge is not); whether Claude Code's submenu ("More models") and its tabs behave as rows and keys here suggest.
- **Not done, and said so.** Tabs are shown, not tappable (the arrows and Tab reach them). A submenu has no "back" of its own beyond the program's Esc. The window has no keys row on a list (the line above the message box has Cancel, and the terminal is one tap away). Rows that are words under a setting ("Thinking can't be turned off…") are drawn as a row; tapping one changes nothing, by the read-back. A shell's own menus are not read. Only what the program's screen shows is listed: at most its window of rows.

#### Any agent, at any time (17 Nov 2026, the owner's question)

"Confirm the picker works on any agent at anytime a multiple choice would come up for any reason". **It did not, and it still does not in every case: what is and is not covered is the table below.** With the real Codex 0.169.0, Antigravity 1.5.9 and OpenCode 2.3.0 each showing its own list of models (captured on a throwaway station), the pane read idle, nothing was on its record, and Chat showed nothing. Why: Codex's and Antigravity's marker is at the very left edge, which the reader took for a program's own message box; OpenCode's list has no marker a row carries (its highlight is a colour).

- **Codex's and Antigravity's lists are read now**, from those captures (kept as fixtures): a marker at the edge (`›`, `>`) is a list's when the rows straight above and below it, with no empty row between, are its choices and a row of key names is under them ("enter select · esc back"; "Keyboard: ↑/↓ Navigate …"). A program's own message box has neither. "(current)" is the one in use; Antigravity's status row under everything is not part of what is shown; its effort row is ◀ ▶ in the window.
- **A net that needs nothing recognised, where a program's ordinary waiting screen is known.** When a pane is not in a turn and the bottom of its screen is known not to be its wait for a message, the pane says "shows something in its terminal", and Chat's window shows the screen itself with the keys and a field to type into it. Known from a real screen, and nothing else: **Antigravity** (its message box is a row `>` between two rules, and waiting, only its status row is under it); **OpenCode** (its windows have a title row that ends, far right, with `esc`). So OpenCode's list of models, and any menu Antigravity draws under its box, are shown though nothing on them can be read. It stands only while the screen is still (its rows are kept with it), never in a turn, and never for the message box with words typed in it.
- **"Not a prompt: hide"** on that window: a wrong guess is one tap, remembered while the screen is exactly that.
- **A screen with key names and nothing marked** is said for any program, in a turn or not, when its row of key names names Enter or Esc and does not say how to interrupt (which is a program at work).
- **Not covered, and said plainly:** a menu of **Claude Code's or Codex's** with no marker a row carries **and** no row of key names (their waiting screens are not captured, so the net above says nothing for them); any program's prompt in the middle of a turn that has neither a marked list nor key names; a shell's menus; a highlight that is only a colour (the screen's cell colours are not read: OpenCode's rows cannot be tapped). The fallback by change alone (the bottom of the screen changed and no record followed) is **not built**.

| | Claude Code | Codex | Antigravity | OpenCode |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Its list of models | window with rows (real capture; run with the real program) | window with rows (real capture; not run) | window with rows (real capture; not run) | screen window (real capture; not run) |
| Another list of a command | rows where it has the structure (`/config`, `/resume`: real captures) | rows if marked with key names (untested) | rows if so, else screen window by the net (untested) | screen window if it has the `esc` title (untested) |
| A question in a turn | its own card, by hooks (real) | rows or screen only with key names (untested) | the same (untested) | the same (untested) |
| A permission prompt | approval card when a hook holds it; in the terminal: "Waiting in the terminal" by the screen rules (real words, no capture of the screen) | the same by its rules (untested) | screen window: a grant (untested) | by its rules (untested) |
| Folder trust | its own card (real) | review of hooks: its own card (real) | its own card (real) | none known |
| A sign-in | "Waiting in the terminal" by the screen rules (untested) | the same (untested) | screen window by the net (untested) | untested |
| An unknown menu | only with a marker or key names | only with a marker or key names | screen window (the net; fixture) | only with the `esc` title |

#### A question can be cancelled, Esc cancels, and the message box is never pushed away (17 Nov 2026, the owner's report from an iPhone)

"I wrote this to Claude, there was no way to cancel, esc didn't work, it also hid the bottom window." A held question's card with four long choices and "Other…" open, the keys row showing, the deck's "new version" bar above: its "Next" was cut off and the message box was half off the screen.

- **"Cancel" on every question card** (`question.answer {id, cancel: true}`, the owner's only, as every answer is). The hook refuses the call with words for the agent: "The owner saw this question in spandeck and chose not to answer it. Do not ask it again as it is: go on as you judge best with what you know, or stop and say what you need." (Claude Code's hook reference: the reason of a refused call is shown to the agent.) The card says what it does. A question that ran out of time or was let go to the terminal still says nothing, as before: only the owner's Cancel declines. "Answer in the terminal instead" is as it was.
- **Why Esc did nothing:** while the station holds a question the program is waiting for its hook and its own dialog is not on its screen, so an Esc typed into the pane reached nothing. **Esc on the keys row now cancels the held question**, and so does the Esc key on a computer while the card has the keyboard. In the window for what a program shows, Esc is the program's own Esc (the window goes with its list); on "Switch model?" the keys row's Esc goes to the program's dialog, which takes it.
- **The message box is never clipped.** The cards above it were as tall as 64 % of the screen whatever else was on it. Now the dock gives way inside itself: the log keeps a strip (44 px), the cards take what is left and scroll, and a held question scrolls its choices with Cancel, Next and "Answer in the terminal instead" always in sight under them. With the keyboard up there is too little room for that: the whole card scrolls and its buttons are reached by scrolling it. The keys row was open because the owner opened it (looking for Esc); it is put away when a new card appears, one tap back.
- **Tests.** The exchange for a cancelled question, and that a lapsed one says nothing (`core/questions.rs`); in a browser at 390×844 (`cli/tests/chat.rs`): four long choices, "Other…" open, the keys row and a bar above: the dock's bottom at the viewport's bottom, every button inside the card and the screen, the choices scrolling; with the keyboard up the message box in place and the buttons reached by scrolling; Esc on the keys row and "Cancel" each telling the agent.
- **Not known:** how the real Claude Code words a refused AskUserQuestion to the model, and whether it then asks again. The bar in the test stands in for the deck's own "new version" bar (same class, same place).

### A pane that may be stuck is told so quietly, and the owner can say it is fine (20 Nov 2026, a fault the owner met)

An agent of the owner's watched long renders: each turn ran a `tail` or two on a log and changed no file. Its pane was marked as needing the owner, "stuck: 8 turns in a row used tools but changed no files", and Chat drew "Waiting in the terminal: stuck: …" with the keys and the program's footer and task list, though nothing was asked. The owner: "why cant I get rid of the terminal at the bottom here, why does it look this way?" It could not be dismissed and came back after every turn: only a turn that changed a file ended the count, and once the count was reached every turn's end raised the reason again, a turn of one tool too.

Stuck detection is a guess, in two kinds (`core/stuck.rs`): **idle turns** (`stuck_idle_turns` turns in a row, 3 unless set, each of five or more tool calls and no file changed) and **the same failure** (`stuck_same_failure` failures in a row of one command within a turn, 3 unless set). The pane's reason says it is a guess and what was counted: "may be stuck: 3 turns in a row ran 5 or more tools each and changed no files", "may be stuck: the same command failed 3 times in a row (make build)". The ship's log has `agent.stuck` as before.

- **What is counted.** A turn that ended with five or more tool calls and no file changed, unless the agent had work running beside it: a sub-agent its hooks named, or anything its conversation lists as running (a sub-agent or a command left in the background, from its session file; a helper pane it started). A turn that waits on such work is not counted and is no new start either. A turn of fewer tools is not counted.
- **What raises the reason.** Only the end of a turn that is itself counted, when the count is at the threshold or over. (Before, any turn's end raised it once the count was reached.)
- **What starts the count again.** A turn that changed a file; the pane's session changing; and **the owner speaking**: a prompt that begins after the owner typed, pasted or sent something into the pane (`pane.input` that is typing, `pane.paste`, `pane.send_text` as the owner: from a deck, a terminal or the command line), with nothing typed into it by anyone else since. The reason goes with that prompt, as with any.
- **What is not the owner speaking.** A nudge for mail, whoever the mail is from, the owner included (the station types it); text the station sends for itself; a prompt that follows no typing at all. The station cannot read from a prompt who wrote it: it goes by who typed into the pane last, and takes the safe side. So a key the owner pressed that began no prompt can make the next prompt count as theirs, once.
- **"It is fine"** (`pane.stuck_fine {pane}`): the owner says the pane is not stuck. The pane leaves "needs you" at once (to "done" for idle turns, "working" for the same failure), and since that is the station's state, on every station and deck. That kind then stays quiet for the pane until its session changes or a file is changed: idle turns altogether, the same failure for that command. It is in the ship's log (`pane.stuck_fine`, with who and from which station). Said of a pane that is not marked stuck, it does nothing and says so (`dismissed: false`). **Rule 7:** it is the owner's alone, on no agent's list, no station's and not the read-only one, and reaches another station as the owner's forwarded request.
- **In Chat** the card "Waiting in the terminal" is for a program that waits at a prompt in its terminal, and is unchanged. A pane said to be stuck has a quiet notice in its place: "watcher may be stuck: 3 turns in a row ran 5 or more tools each and changed no files. Nothing waits for an answer.", with **It is fine** and **Open terminal**: none of the terminal's lines, no keys. Both kinds are shown so.
- **Elsewhere** the reason is the pane's `needs_reason` as any other: the left list, the station's tile, a phone's list, the dot's title, the Inbox notice ("watcher needs you", with the reason under it). The pane's strip in Terminal view has **It is fine** beside the reason, and the pane's menu "It is fine (not stuck)".
- **What a station says:** `stuck` in a pane's view and in `live` of its conversation: `null`, or `{kind: "idle_turns" | "same_failure", says}` while the pane needs the owner for that reason.
- **Mixed versions.** A deck from before shows the new reason in the old card, with the terminal's lines and keys, and has no button. A station from before says no `stuck`: a deck of today shows its panes as before. A pane's record from before that still holds "stuck: …" is known for what it is.
- **Tests.** Every change: the counting rule as the plain function it is (`core/stuck.rs`), and who may call (`core/rpc.rs`). Merge tier (`cli/tests/stuck.rs`, with the stand-in agent): the reason raised, cleared by the owner's prompt and the count started again, and not by a nudge for mail; three turns with a command running in the background not counted, and counted once it is over; "It is fine" from another station's deck, refused to an agent, both stations agreeing, quiet for two more turns and watched again after a file is changed; in a browser at a phone's width, the notice for both kinds with no terminal lines and no keys in sight, its button, and the old card with its lines and keys for a program that waits in its terminal.
- **Not done, and said so.** Nothing of this was run against the real Claude Code. Whether its prompt hook runs for a turn it begins by itself (a background task reporting in) is not known here: if it does, that prompt is nobody's typing and leaves the count as it is. A command the session file never reports over keeps counting as running, and stuck detection for idle turns stays off for that pane until the session changes. The same failure is counted within one turn only, as before.

### The microphone (17 Nov 2026, the owner's report from an iPhone)

The owner: **"Can you research how Claude or ChatGPT use the microphone, I tapped it on a spark2 session and it locked up spandeck I had to close the web page and restart. So they use on device on Apple, do they run their own? What should we do?"**

- **What the button was.** The browser's own dictation (`SpeechRecognition`, in Safari `webkitSpeechRecognition`), asked to listen without end (`continuous`) and to report as it goes (`interimResults`), each report rewriting the whole message box. The button was "on" from `start()` until the browser said `onend`, and only the browser could end it.
- **What locked up: not reproduced, and not known.** No iPhone can be driven from a station, and no source describes Safari freezing a page at `start()`. What reading the code and WebKit's own sources gives:
  - *Certain (faults in our code, each fixed):* nothing ended a session but the browser's `onend`, so a browser that never says it (reported of iOS with `continuous`: results that are never final, listening that goes on after `stop()`) left the microphone open with no limit, the button doing nothing, and **every report writing over whatever the owner typed in the box**; a start that was answered by nothing was waited for for ever; each report cost two layouts of the page; a hold started dictation from a timer, and a finger whose lifting the page never heard of made the next tap do nothing.
  - *Likely, from those:* on the HTTPS deck (where Safari 26 has the interface; at the plain address it has none, and the old button only said so) dictation began and never ended, and the message box could not be used.
  - *Possible, and outside our code:* Safari itself stopping the page while it asks for the microphone and for speech recognition, or while its speech service does not answer (it is not offered at all to a page opened from the home screen: WebKit bug 225298). The page now says what happened, so the next report is not a guess.
  - *Read and found not to be it:* the fail-over and the heartbeat (a page whose clock stood still was "away", and nothing is switched or reloaded for it; a box with text in it is never taken to another station), the service worker, the redraw every few seconds (the box is kept), and the keyboard's focus. Nothing in the old path disabled the box, covered the page or captured the pointer.
- **Now (`mic.js`, one piece for the chat's box and the terminal's).** This file's own state decides what the button is, never an event that may not come.
  - A start that throws is said in words. A start answered by nothing within **1.5 s** is given up and said (**30 s** while the browser may be asking the owner for permission: its permission is "ask", or the window lost focus; the button is Stop meanwhile).
  - **The button stops whatever is going on, at once, in every state.** The browser is told, and is not waited for: its last words are taken for 1.5 s and then it is cut off.
  - A session ends by itself after **two minutes**; when the page is hidden or its window is left; when the finger that held the button is lifted anywhere, or is lost (the next tap stops it); and **when the owner types in the box**, which is never written over.
  - What is heard is written at most eight times a second, however often the browser reports.
  - Nothing is disabled, covered or focused from here. A line by the box says what is going on and why it could not: "The microphone is not available on this address: a browser gives it only to a secure (HTTPS) page" with **Open the secure deck** when the station has one; "Dictation was not allowed" and where the setting is; "This browser has no dictation"; each with "Use the microphone key on the keyboard".
  - **On a page that is not secure, or a browser without dictation, nothing is tried.**
- **What the next report will say.** Every attempt is told to the station (`client.report`, `what: "mic"`): what was tried (`engine`), by a tap or a hold, each event the browser sent with its time in milliseconds, how it ended (`ended`), the error's name, the permission, secure or not, from the home screen or not, in the app's shell or not, and the browser's family. **A session whose page did not live to end it is told by the next page that opens** (`died: true`, with its last state, its events and for how long the page was alive after the tap). And a page whose own clock missed more than four seconds while in sight says so when it answers again (`what: "page-stall"`, with the microphone's state, the view, and what had the keyboard). Read with `spandeck rpc log.query '{"kind":"client.report"}'`.
- **How the others do it** (read 17 Nov 2026; what could not be read is said):
  - *Anthropic.* Dictation in the Claude apps sends the sound to Anthropic's servers ("Nothing is transcribed on your device"; "a contracted speech-to-text subprocessor", not named; the recording is deleted after it is text, unless the owner opted in to share it): support.claude.com/en/articles/10065434, claude.com/docs/office-agents/dictation.md. Voice mode likewise keeps no audio by default (support.claude.com/en/articles/11101966). Claude Code's `/voice` "streams your recorded audio to Anthropic's servers… Audio is not processed locally" (code.claude.com/docs/en/voice-dictation). The Mac app's Quick Entry dictation asks for macOS's Speech Recognition permission, which suggests Apple's recognizer there (an inference). Anthropic's API offers no speech to text.
  - *OpenAI.* ChatGPT's dictation is "sent to our models to be transcribed" and kept with the chat; voice mode is speech-to-speech on their servers over WebRTC (help.openai.com/en/articles/12168547 and 8400625, seen as search excerpts only: the pages refused to load; livekit.com/blog/openai-livekit-partnership-advanced-voice-realtime-api, Oct 2024).
  - **So: neither uses Apple's on-device speech for it; both record and transcribe on their own servers.** On-device is what the iPhone's keyboard does, and what an app may ask Apple for.
  - *Safari's Web Speech API.* Since 14.1; since 26.0 only on a secure page (webkit.org/blog/17333, bug 292941); Apple's recognizer, on the device only where it supports the language there and otherwise at Apple's servers; needs Siri & Dictation on; aborts when the page is hidden (bug 220073); "not available in SafariViewController and web apps added to Home Screen" (bug 225298, 2021, closed without a fix; not verified on iOS 26, where every saved site opens as a web app); no control of punctuation.
  - *The keyboard's microphone key.* On the device in many languages, punctuates by itself, in any text field of any page or app, and nothing a page does (support.apple.com/guide/iphone/iph2c0651d2).
  - *A recording* (`getUserMedia` and `MediaRecorder`) needs a secure page; Safari records MP4/AAC, and WebM/Opus since 18.4. A page saved to the home screen is asked for the microphone again each time it is opened (bug 215884).
- **The ways, compared.** A: the browser's dictation, made safe (done). B: the keyboard's key. C: record, and a station's own program makes the text. D: record, and a provider makes the text through AgentVault. E: Apple's on-device speech in the native app.

  | | Quality | Wait | Where the sound goes | Cost | HTTP | HTTPS | Home screen | Native shell | Computer | Work |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | A | fair; no punctuation control | as you speak | Apple (or Google in Chrome); on the device only for some languages | none | no | yes | no (WebKit says) | only with the app's usage strings | Chrome, Edge, Safari; not Firefox | done |
  | B | good; punctuates | as you speak | on the phone | none | yes | yes | yes | yes | no (macOS: press the dictation key) | none |
  | C | best (large Whisper, Parakeet) | 1 to 3 s after you stop (expected, not measured) | **a station of the fleet, over the tailnet, deleted at once** | none | no | yes | yes, asked again each launch | yes | yes | seam built; a model to install |
  | D | best | 1 to 3 s after you stop | the provider; kept as its terms say | about $0.003 to $0.006 a minute | no | yes | yes | yes | yes | a vault route, a few days |
  | E | good to very good (iOS 26 SpeechTranscriber) | as you speak | on the phone | none | n/a | n/a | n/a | **yes** | Mac app | in the app: a week |

- **The order (the owner's expectation, which the research confirms).** (1) A is made safe now and is offered only where it can work. (2) **B is the way on an iPhone today**: it is the only one that works at the plain address and from the home screen, it is on the device, and it costs nothing; the button says so wherever it cannot work itself. (3) E is the native app's voice: the same button, through the app's bridge. (4) C is the private way for browsers and computers: the seam is built and off; it becomes the button's way wherever a station says it can. (5) D only if C's quality or a station's absence asks for it.
- **One deliberate act, in every one of them.** What is said becomes text in the box and nothing else: it is read, corrected and sent by the owner, with Send, as a typed message is (`sendAct`). A: the words appear as they are heard. B: the keyboard writes them. C and D: they arrive together after "Transcribing on spark2…", after whatever is in the box by then. E: as A. **Nothing that listens ever sends**, and a later "send when I stop talking" would be its own decision.
- **C, the seam (built, off by default).** `[speech] command = ["whisper-cli", "-m", "…", "-nt", "-np", "-f", "{file}"]` in a station's `config.toml` (the program and its arguments, run as written with no shell; `{file}` is the recording, or it is the last argument; it prints the text). With it the station says `speech` in its `caps` and answers **`speech.transcribe`** `{data_b64, mime, seconds}` → `{text, ms, station}`: the owner's alone (no agent, no station acting for itself, no read-only caller: `rpc.rs`), one recording at a time, at most `max_bytes` (4 MB) and `max_seconds` (60, as the page says it; the station cannot hear the length, so the size and the time are the limits that hold), a recording's own kinds only (`audio/mp4`, `audio/webm`, `audio/ogg`, `audio/wav`, `audio/mpeg`); the file is written under the state directory readable by the station's user only, the program is given `timeout_secs` (60) and then ended, and **the file is deleted whatever happened**. The log says that a recording was transcribed, its size and time, and nothing of what was said. It runs beside the page's other calls (it holds up no typing). **spandeck ships no model and calls no service**: where the sound goes is where that program sends it.
  - *The page.* Where the pane's station (or the page's own, or any station of the fleet) says `speech`, and the page is secure and can record, the button records instead (64 kbit/s, at most a minute): "Recording, for spark2 to turn into text. Tap the microphone when you are done", then "Transcribing on spark2… Tap the microphone to cancel", then the text in the box. The same watchdog, Stop, limits and reports.
  - *Which program* (from their own pages, none run here): on spark2 (arm64 Linux, NVIDIA) whisper.cpp built with CUDA and `large-v3-turbo`, or Parakeet through NeMo (English `tdt-0.6b-v2`, 25 languages `v3`); faster-whisper's arm64 package has no CUDA (a 20 s clip took 40 to 100 s on the CPU in one report), so not that; on the Mac whisper.cpp with Metal or Core ML; on rtx6000 faster-whisper or whisper.cpp with CUDA. whisper.cpp reads 16-bit WAV: the command is a two-line script that runs `ffmpeg` first, or a build with `WHISPER_COMMON_FFMPEG`. A model that stays loaded (`whisper-server`) answers faster than one started for each clip; the command can be a `curl` to it on localhost.
  - *Not built:* choosing the station with the capability by latency (the first that says it can is used: the pane's, then the page's, then any); a recording longer than a minute; text as you speak.
- **D, if it is wanted.** A vault route that carries a file to a transcription endpoint, so that the key is never on a station or the phone. OpenAI (`gpt-transcribe` $0.0045 a minute; `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe` $0.003; `whisper-1` $0.006, to be shut down 26 Feb 2027; 25 MB; m4a and webm taken; not trained on), Groq (`whisper-large-v3-turbo` $0.04 an hour), Deepgram Nova-3 ($0.0043 a minute), ElevenLabs Scribe ($0.20 an hour), AssemblyAI ($0.15 to $0.21 an hour). Not Anthropic: its API has none.
- **E, the native app** (a brief for `~/code/spandeck-app`; not built here). The app offers `spandeckShell.speech` to the page, and the same button uses it before anything else: `available()` → `{onDevice, languages}`, `start({lang, hold})`, `stop()`, `cancel()`, and events to the page `{type: "start" | "partial" | "final" | "error" | "end", text, error}`. iOS 26: `SpeechAnalyzer` with `SpeechTranscriber` (on the device, long-form; its model is fetched once by `AssetInventory`), falling back to `DictationTranscriber`, and before iOS 26 to `SFSpeechRecognizer` with `requiresOnDeviceRecognition = true` where `supportsOnDeviceRecognition` (never the server: "audio never leaves the phone" is the promise). `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription` and `NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription` in Info.plist; no entitlement is known to be needed (from memory: to be checked in Xcode). The audio session is `.record` with `.duckOthers`, given back at `end`. The app keeps the same rules as the page: a watchdog on start, a two-minute limit, an end when it goes to the background, and **text only into the box**.
- **Not run:** on an iPhone, in Safari, from the home screen or in the app's shell: every part of this is by construction and with stand-ins for the browser's dictation and recorder (`tests/mic.rs`). A real speech program. The offer of the secure deck from the microphone's line (the line is tested; the button beside it is the one the fleet view has). The terminal face's microphone in a browser test (it is the same piece). Windows: the seam compiles there and its scenario test is Unix's (a shell script stands in for the program).

#### Run with the four real programs, and the net for Claude Code and Codex (17 Nov 2026)

Each real program was taken through: waiting for a message, the same with words typed in, its list of models, one arrow down, a message sent meanwhile, Esc (Claude Code 2.1.326, Codex 0.169.0, Antigravity 1.5.10, OpenCode 2.3.0). **Proven there:** each one's wait, empty and with a draft, says nothing; Claude Code's, Codex's and Antigravity's lists are windows with rows and the marker follows the arrow; OpenCode's is the screen window; the message is held in all four; after Esc each is as it was. **Found there:** an idle Antigravity was taken for one showing something (the row under its box has a hint at the left and its status at the right on one row) and every message to it was held: fixed on the day. And on the owner's own station a Codex pane that had had `/hooks` open for a day now says so.

- **Claude Code and Codex have the net now.** Their ordinary wait is known from those captures: Claude Code's message box (a row beginning `❯` between two rules; what it draws under the box while a command is typed is still its wait), Codex's row beginning `› ` with at most its two status rows under it. **Once a pane has shown that wait since its program started**, something else in its place while it is not in a turn is "shows something in its terminal", with the screen window. (Only once it has been seen: a program still starting, or one whose screen was never read, is never taken for one showing something.)
- **In the middle of a turn**, for those two: the same, when the screen has stood still for ten seconds. A program at work keeps drawing (its timer, its spinner); one that has stopped to ask does not.
- **The question card** has the rows of the window for a program's list: one framed list, rows of 52 px, the chosen one filled.
- **Not built:** telling by change alone for a program with no known screen at all (there is none among the four); tappable tabs; a submenu's own way back (the program's Esc). OpenCode's wait is known only as "no window with an `esc` title": its input panel was not made a rule, since its screen during a session is not captured.

| | Claude Code | Codex | Antigravity | OpenCode |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Waiting, empty or with a draft: nothing | **real** | **real** | **real** (after the fix) | **real** |
| Its list of models | rows; **real**, a tap chosen for the session | rows, marker follows; **real** (no tap made) | rows, marker follows; **real** (no tap made) | screen window; **real** |
| A message held meanwhile | **real** | **real** | **real** | **real** |
| Another list of a command | rows (`/config`, `/resume`: real screens, stand-in run) | `/hooks`: screen window, **real**; others by structure | rows or screen window (stand-in) | screen window if it has the `esc` title (stand-in) |
| An unknown menu, not in a turn | screen window by the net (stand-in) | screen window by the net (stand-in) | screen window (stand-in) | only with the `esc` title |
| An unknown menu in a turn | after ten still seconds (not tested) | after ten still seconds (not tested) | only with key names | only with key names |
| A question in a turn | its own card, by hooks (**real**) | by key names or the ten seconds (untested) | by key names (untested) | by key names (untested) |
| A permission prompt in the terminal | by the screen rules' words (no capture) | no capture | no capture | no capture |
| Folder trust, review of hooks | own card (**real**) | own card (**real**) | own card (**real**) | none known |
| A sign-in | untested | untested | untested | untested |

### A photo or a document goes with the message (17 Nov 2026, the owner's request)

"In the chat I want to add a photo or document to the agent just like Claude or open ai, build test and release". Until now the "+" of the message box saved a file on the pane's station and **at once typed its path into the program**. `crates/core/src/attach.rs` (new), `chat.js`; the calls are in `docs/conversation-api.md`. Three new methods, the owner's only; no migration (the record is in the station's key-value table, for each pane); nothing new for an agent. The same for every agent and every program, on a phone and on a computer.

- **A file waits in the message box, and goes with the message.** Chosen from "+" (a photo or a file, or the camera: as before on an iPhone), **pasted** into the box, or **dropped** on the conversation (the place to drop is shown), it is a chip in a row above what is written: a picture small and rounded (64 px, from the device's own copy), a document a tile with its kind, its name and its size; an × in the corner; how far it has got while it goes to the station; "not sent" and Retry when it did not arrive. Several at once: ten with a message, 64 MB each, any kind. **Nothing is typed until Send**, and Send is off while a file is still on its way. Files alone are a message; a message with no file is as it always was.
- **One message goes to the agent:** the owner's words, then a line for each file: `Attached file: <its whole path on the pane's station>`. **No program's own way of taking a file is established** (Claude Code is said to read a picture whose path is in the prompt; nothing was read for Codex, Antigravity or OpenCode), so every program is given the same line, which any of them can open as a file. It is `pane.paste` with `files`: one deliberate act, refused whole when a file is not on the station (taken off, or it never arrived), so **a path is never typed for a file that is not there**; and refused, like any message, while the program shows a question or a list of its own.
- **Where a file is kept.** At the pane's own station (through another station's deck the pieces are passed on, as every call is), under the station's state in a folder of the pane's own (`drops/<pane>/attached/`), under a name made of an id and the device's name made safe (letters, digits, `.`, `-`, `_`; no leading dot), never over another file, readable by the owner's account alone (0600). Two hundred a pane; **they go when the pane is closed.** No other retention: a pane kept for months keeps its last two hundred.
- **It travels in pieces** (180 KB each, one call a piece, one after another), so no single call is large and a key typed, a Stop or an answer given meanwhile is not kept waiting behind a photo. (Not measured against a key's echo with a 20 MB file.)
- **In the conversation** the owner's message shows what went with it: pictures as thumbnails (a tap shows one large), documents as tiles with name and size; the line the station added is not in the bubble. The station knows which files went with which message, so it is the same after a reload, on another device and through another station's deck. It finds the message by the file's path in its words; where the program rewrote the path (Claude Code takes a picture in as "[Image #1]"), by the message it was sent with: within thirty seconds, beginning with the same words. A picture is fetched by its pane and its id (`pane.attach.get`), from the station's own record: **no path comes from the page**, and only a file attached for that pane is ever read.
- **Who may (rule 7).** `pane.attach.put`, `.get` and `.remove` are on no agent's list, no station's and not the read-only one.
- **A draft's files** stay through redraws and between Chat and Terminal, as its words do. **A reload loses the files that were not sent** (the page's own copies are gone; what had reached the station stays there, unsent, until the pane is closed).
- **Not changed:** the terminal face's own paste and drop (`pane.drop_file`), which type the path at once, as a terminal does.
- **Mixed versions.** A station from before has none of the three calls: a chip says that station is from before files could go with a message, and nothing is sent. A deck from before on a station of today: its "+" is the old one.
- **Several files go one after another, and a piece that fails is tried again** (22 Nov 2026). The owner, of three screenshots of 3 MB or so from a phone to a station on another network: "I tried to upload 3 pics and they were slow and finally errored", twice. On the station were the first 2.4 to 2.6 MB of each, all three ended in the same second. The page had sent all three at once, and the first piece of a file that got no answer ended that file, which then began again from nothing (leaving the part behind). So: files to one pane go up one at a time; a piece begins at 45 KB and doubles to 180 KB only while a piece is answered within 0.4 s, and halves when one takes over 2 s; a piece that fails is tried again 8 times over about 50 s, each time first asking the station how much of the file it has (an empty piece is answered with the size) and carrying on from there; "Retry" carries on from there too; the chip says the station did not answer and that it is trying again. An upload the station no longer takes pieces for is removed there and begun again. On the station, one change to a pane's record of files is made at a time: pieces arrive on threads of their own, and two that read the record and wrote it back could lose one another's. **Not established:** which limit ended the owner's uploads (the page's connection being given up, or a piece from one station to the other not answered in its 5 s): no station keeps a record of a piece that failed. Not tried on a real phone. Test (merge tier, a browser): three files of 400 KB given together, the browser's socket closed in place of the fourth piece, the pieces of one file never among another's, and all three on the station byte for byte with nothing left half sent.
- **Tests.** Every change: a name made safe, the line (`core/attach.rs`); who may call (`core/rpc.rs`); one station scenario (`cli/tests/chat.rs`, 0.4 s): put in pieces and nothing typed, the name and mode on disk, read back by pane and id only, every agent refused, a removed file never named, one message with the line, the conversation's item with its attachments and its words alone, the files gone with the pane. Merge tier, in a browser at 390×844 and on a computer: a picture pasted and documents dropped as chips, one removed, Send sending one message, the bubble with the picture after a reload, files alone, the chips with the keyboard up.
- **Not known, and wanting the real programs:** what each does with the line (reads the picture, reads the document, or only sees a path); how each writes the message to its own record (whether the line survives, and so whether the bubble is matched by path or by time); whether a path of this length is typed whole into each. **Not tested:** a failed upload and Retry in a browser; a 20 MB file.

#### Antigravity beginning its turn (17 Nov 2026, found with the real 1.5.10)

For some eight seconds after a message was sent to it, the pane read "shows something in its terminal": it draws under its message box as its turn begins, before its state is "working". Nothing unread is said of an Antigravity pane in the twenty seconds after the owner sent it something, nor of any pane whose screen was drawn on in the last second and a half. (A list it shows is still read at once: that is by structure, not by this.)

### What an agent makes is shown (17 Nov 2026, the owner's request)

"Can I paste or add an image in ChatGPT or Gemini and have it make a image or video for me, would that work in out chat?" Then: "Ok test it out and see if we can add, then test and release". Tried with the real programs that day (a picture attached, "make a watercolour of it with your built-in tool"): **Codex 0.169** has a tool for it and saved a PNG under `~/.codex/generated_images/…`, its reply saying `Path: /home/…/exec-….png`; **Antigravity 1.5.10** saved a JPEG under its `brain/<conversation>/` and its reply had the path in backticks and a link `(file:///…)`; **Claude Code** has no such tool (it can still write a picture by a script). Video was not tried: none offered it. **The owner saw a path as text.** `crates/core/src/made.rs` (new), `chat.js`; the calls are in `docs/conversation-api.md`. Two new methods, the owner's only; no migration; nothing new for an agent.

- **A picture or a video an agent's reply names is shown under the reply.** When the station builds a conversation answer it looks through what the agent said (and what a pane printed, and a tool call's one line) for names of files on that station: whole paths, `~/…`, `file://` addresses, and names relative to the pane's folder, ending `.png .jpg .jpeg .webp .gif` or `.mp4 .webm .mov .m4v`, out of backticks, brackets and links. One that is a plain file there (links followed to the real file) **and whose first bytes are a picture or a video: never by its name**, up to 40 MB for a picture and 300 MB for a video, is put on the item (`media`) under an id of the station's own. Eight on one reply, the rest counted; the same file named twice is shown once. Never from the owner's own message. A name with a blank in it is found only in a `file://` address (`%20`).
