- **What this lets an agent's text do, and no more (rule 6).** An agent's text is data. Here it chooses which picture or video file on the pane's station **the owner's** deck is shown: a file that agent's own program could already read, and only one that is a picture or a video by its bytes. A file named `notes.png` that is text is not served; a link named as a picture that leads elsewhere is not; an address on the web is never fetched. `pane.media.get` and `pane.media.attach` take the station's id, never a path, and are on no agent's list, no station's and not the read-only one.
- **In the bubble**, in the deck's own theme: one picture large, with its own shape, to the bubble's width; several as a grid; fetched when they come near what is in sight. A tap shows it large (a tap again to look closer; the phone's own pinch works on it), with **Save** (the phone's share sheet where it takes a file, else a download under the file's name), **Copy path**, and **Attach to my next message**. A video is a tile with its name and size: a tap loads it, saying how far, and it plays where it is (`<video controls playsinline>`). The agent's words stay as it wrote them, path and all.
- **"Attach to my next message"** is the loop the owner asked about ("now make it night-time"): the station makes the picture a file of the owner's next message itself (it has it: nothing goes to the phone and back), and it is a chip in the message box like any other.
- **A video travels as a picture does** (pieces of 256 KB over the deck's own connection, into the page's memory), so it can be played only once it is all there, and one of hundreds of megabytes is slow and heavy on a phone: its size is said before the tap. (Asking the station's web side for a part of a file, which would let a video play before it has all arrived, was not looked into: it would be a second road to these files with its own check of who asks, and through another station's deck.) **The deck's content policy now lets the page play a video from bytes it holds** (`media-src 'self' blob:`), as it already drew pictures from them; nothing from another origin is allowed.
- **The files stay where the agent wrote them**: nothing is copied (but for "Attach", into the message's own folder), moved or deleted. One that is gone later is said to be gone, in its place.
- **Mixed versions.** A station from before sends no `media`: the path is text, as before. A deck from before ignores `media`.
- **Antigravity's own rows (found the same day with 1.5.10).** After a turn that used a sub-agent, a row `● Agent(…)` and a rule stand between its message box and its status row, which then also says `· 1 subagent(s)`: the pane read "shows something in its terminal". Those rows are its own. The rule "nothing is said of an Antigravity pane for twenty seconds after the owner sent it something" is taken out again: what it was written for had another cause (a lone "esc to cancel" under its box while it works), fixed where it was.
- **In a narrow terminal its hint stands alone (24 Nov 2026).** The owner's picture, from the phone: under a finished answer a card "Antigravity shows something in its terminal: its own screen" that "won't go away and doesn't seem to mean anything". The pane's terminal was 43 columns wide, and there Antigravity (1.5.10) draws only `? for shortcuts` under its message box, with no status beside it; the rule for its own row wanted both on one row. A row under the box that is exactly `? for shortcuts` or `esc to cancel` is its own. Tried on LLM-Server with 0.4.25-rc1, the card was still there: the pane was by then 60 columns wide, where the row is the hint, tabs, `auto · Gemini 4 Neon`, and the rest of the status (` · high`) on a row of its own. So: a row under the box that begins with either hint is its own, and so is a short row begun with `·`. With 0.4.25-rc2 it was still there: the same pane, the same width, the status now cut as `…auto · Gemin` / `i 4 Neon · high` (the tabs before it had widened). So the rule no longer reads the pieces: under the box, the row that begins with a hint and the one or two short rows after it are the status, however it is cut; the test cuts the row at every place. *Seen:* idle screens at 43 columns and, cut two ways, at 60. **Not seen:** what it draws under the box at that width while it works, or with a sub-agent listed.
- **Tests.** Every change: the names found in the replies' real shapes and what is not one; a picture or video by its bytes, a text file named as one, a link elsewhere, `..` (`core/made.rs`); who may call (`core/rpc.rs`); one station scenario (`cli/tests/chat.rs`, 0.3 s): `media` on the reply only, read by id, another pane's id, a made-up id and a path refused, every agent refused, attached to the next message, and gone. Merge tier, in a browser at 390×844 and on a computer, with a real picture and a real second of video (drawn and recorded by the browser, written where the reply says): the picture drawn, large with its three actions, the video loaded and ready to play, the picture sent back as a chip and its path reaching the program, the same after a reload.
- **Not known, and wanting the real programs:** that Codex's and Antigravity's replies are found as they really write them (the fixtures follow the two replies seen); a JPEG's and a WebP's shape before it is loaded (only a PNG says its size in its first bytes here); a real video from an agent (kind, size, whether a phone plays its codec); Save on an iPhone (the share sheet at a plain `http://` address).
- **Not done, and said so.** A path line shortened to the file's name; a video's poster picture and its length before it is loaded; playing a video before it has all arrived; pictures an agent shows only on its terminal (inline image protocols); a name with blanks outside a `file://` address.

### Files an agent sent are shown in Chat (21 Nov 2026, the owner's request)

A bug that happened. Claude Code in a pane "sent" files to the owner twelve times with its own tool, `SendUserFile` (input `{"files": [paths], "caption"}`; its result says "N files delivered to user."). Nothing appeared in Chat, which the owner was reading on an iPhone: the tool hands files to Claude's own app, and spandeck showed a folded tool step. `crates/archive/src/conversation.rs`, `crates/web/assets/chat.js`; `docs/conversation-api.md`, `sent`.

- **The step carries what was sent.** A `SendUserFile` call is still a `tool` item, with the files' names as its one line, and has `sent`: the caption, up to twelve files (`path`, `name`) and how many more the call named. A path that is not whole is taken from the folder the record says the agent was in. The station looks at no file for this. It is read from the session file like the rest, so an ended pane's sends and an older conversation's are there too. A station that sends it says `sent_files` in `caps`; from one that does not, the step has no `sent` and Chat shows the tool step as before. No method is added, no migration, `PROTOCOL` unchanged.
- **Chat shows a card at that step**, under the run of tool calls it is in: "The agent sent you N files", the caption, and each file by its name with its size. A picture is drawn small and a tap opens it; a film or a sound has Play; anything else has Open; each has Download. Open and Play are the Files preview (above, "A film and a sound play in the preview"). A file that is not there says "It is not on <station> any more." and has no buttons. Beyond twelve: "and N more". A send that failed, was denied or was stopped has no card.
- **The files are asked for as the owner, on the pane's station**, with the calls the Files panel makes: `files.stat`, and `files.ticket` (with `station` when the pane is on another station than the page came from). So what a file is is decided by the station from its first bytes: a page named `.png` is never drawn, and one named `.mp4` is never played. The `files.` methods stay the owner's alone; nothing was added to the agents' list, the stations' list or the read-only list.
- **What this lets an agent's text do (rule 6).** The caption, the names and the paths are an agent's text and go on the page as text. A path chooses which file on the pane's station the owner's deck asks about, a file the owner could open in the Files panel anyway; it is shown only to the owner, and only when the owner's browser asks. A deck with no `files` on that station says the station needs updating.
- **Not built**: other programs' tools of the kind (none was seen); a card in a sub-agent's own conversation; sending a file back from the card; marking a file the owner has opened.
- **Not verified**: a real Claude Code session (the record's shape is from the owner's report, written by the stand-in agent); Safari and a real iPhone.

**Tests**: the call read into `sent`, the twelve and the rest, a call that names no file (`crates/archive/src/conversation.rs`); a real browser at a phone's size with the page from one station and the pane on another (`crates/cli/tests/chat.rs`): the card and its rows, a caption and a name full of markup as text with nothing run, a picture drawn and a page named `.png` not, Play answered in parts through the first station, Open, Download's link, a file that is gone, fourteen files, a failed send, a reload, a computer's width, and the words' contrast in the four themes.

## The folder-trust question (16 Nov 2026, the owner's request)

"I thought you were building a auto approve when a agent starts to trust this folder question." It was not being built; it is now. Until this, an agent program started in a folder it had not seen stopped at its own question and the pane sat at "needs you" until someone answered in its terminal. `crates/agents/src/foldertrust.rs` (reads a screen, presses nothing), `crates/core/src/foldertrust.rs` (whose choice, and the key). No migration is added. One line is added to the Guarantees, under Enforcement, and the line above it there now names this as an exception (since the same day there are two: see Trusting spandeck's own hooks).

**The rule.** The owner's own act of starting an agent in a folder, with a visible and remembered choice, is the consent. spandeck then answers that one question, once, by pressing the key the owner would press, and never anything else.

- **Where the choice is made.** A tick in the New pane dialog and in the phone's New session sheet, "Trust this folder for <agent> if it asks", shown only for a program whose question spandeck can read (Claude Code, Antigravity, and Codex since 18 Nov 2026), with the folder's path, what it allows in that program's own documented words, and the warning below. `spandeck pane new --trust-folder` is the same tick. **Off for a folder never used.** Ticked, it is remembered for that folder and that program on that station, and the tick starts on there next time; unticked at a later start, it is forgotten. A caller that sends no tick (the command line without the flag, a deck from before this, a project's launcher) gets the standing choice as it is.
- **A project's switch**, "Agents may be started in this project's folders without asking me to trust them" (the project's menu, "Folder trust for this project"). **Off until the owner turns it on**, per project and per station. On, it covers the project's folder, folders under it, and the worktrees and snapshots spandeck makes for that project's helpers.
- **Where the choices live.** In the station's own state (`folder_trust` in its key-value store), because a folder is a machine's. **Never in `.spandeck/project.toml`**: that file travels with the repository, and a repository must not be able to say that it is trusted. Listed, with Remove, in Agent setup ("Folders trusted for agents on <station>") and in the project's dialog. Removing one stops spandeck answering; what the program recorded for itself is the program's own and is not touched.
- **Who may use it (rule 7).** `pane.spawn`, `pane.trust_folder`, `foldertrust.choice`, `foldertrust.list`, `foldertrust.remove` and `foldertrust.project` are the owner's: on no agent's list, no station's and not the read-only one; they reach another station only as the owner's forwarded request, and what is remembered is remembered at the station the pane runs on. The choice on a pane is worked out by whoever starts it and is never read from a caller's parameters. A pane an agent asks for (`delegate`) is answered only inside a project the owner switched on, whatever the agent sends; with the switch off it waits for the owner like any other.
- **What is answered.** Only when all of these hold: the pane was started with the choice, and the choice still stands when the key would be pressed (a folder taken off the list, or a project switched off, is not answered); the screen is that program's own prompt **word for word, from the first thing on the screen to the last, naming the pane's own folder** (the shapes below; a line wrapped by a narrower terminal is the same words); it was first seen within 20 seconds of the program's start and before any hook of the session; it has stood unchanged for 0.7 seconds; the program's marker is **on the trusting choice** (where the program puts it on "No, exit", spandeck moves it first and reads the screen again: see "Fully automatic" below); the owner has not typed into the pane; and nothing was answered in this pane since the program started. Then Enter is sent, as one item of the pane's input (`terminal/input.rs`). Once per start of the program.
- **What is never answered** by this rule: Codex's "Hooks need review" (that is about hooks, not a folder, and has a rule of its own: Trusting spandeck's own hooks), a permission prompt, a login, "Allow calling this tool?", a question or a plan, the same words printed later in a session, a second question in the same pane, another program's screen, a screen for another folder, and **a screen that says anything more than the known one**. Claude Code adds lines to its question when the folder's own settings would pre-approve tools, add folders or run helper commands ("These will apply without asking. Only proceed if you trust this configuration."): that is exactly the case the owner should read, and it is left to them.
- **The programs' own trust files are never written** (`~/.claude.json`, `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json`, `~/.codex/config.toml`): a key is pressed, and the program records what it records.
- **When nothing is pressed** the pane says "asks whether to trust this folder: answer in the terminal" (its `needs_reason`; `trust.asks` in its view says why: `not_chosen`, `own_choice_is_no`, `not_the_known_screen`, `switched_off`), and where spandeck can read the two choices the deck offers **Trust this folder** on the pane, with "remember" beside it on a phone and in the pane's menu on a desktop (`pane.trust_folder`). That is the owner's own tap: it moves the program's marker to the trusting choice if it is not there, reads the screen again, and presses Enter only when the marker is seen on "Yes, I trust this folder"; otherwise it says that nothing was confirmed.
- **On record.** `pane.trust_answered` in the ship's log: the pane, the folder, the program, `how` (`standing` or `tap`), who started the pane and why the choice stood (`folder` or `project`). The pane says "spandeck answered <program>'s folder-trust question, as you chose for this folder" for ten minutes. No notice is raised for a question spandeck answered. `foldertrust.remembered`, `foldertrust.removed` and `foldertrust.project` record the choices.
- **Restart.** A program started again in the same pane (the owner's Restart, an agent update, the station after a crash) is treated the same way, and only for a pane that was started with the choice, which is on the pane's record. spandeck answering lifts no Pause and no hold; the owner's own tap counts as the owner typing into the pane, which releases a hold as any key of theirs does, and never ends a Pause.

**The screens, and where each comes from.** None is a guess.

- **Claude Code.** Recorded from 2.1.323 (`crates/terminal/recordings/claude-code-tui.110x36.bin`, 13 Nov 2026), and the same words and options in the program text of 2.1.325 (its `TrustDialog`): a rule, "Accessing workspace:", the folder, "Quick safety check: Is this a project you created or one you trust? (Like your own code, a well-known open source project, or work from your team). If not, take a moment to review what's in this folder first.", "Claude Code'll be able to read, edit, and execute files here.", "Security guide", then "❯ No, exit" above "Yes, I trust this folder", and "Enter to confirm · Esc to cancel". **Its own choice is "No, exit"** (`cancelFirst`, `focus: "cancel"` in the program), it takes no letter as a shortcut, and it takes no key in the first moment the prompt is up. Enter alone leaves the program. **So as first built spandeck pressed nothing for Claude Code**: the tick was remembered, the dialog said so, and the pane offered the one tap. This was found while building it; the owner's request assumed Enter accepts. The owner decided the same day that spandeck moves the marker itself ("Fully automatic" below).
- **Antigravity.** The owner's capture on 1.5.9 (16 Nov 2026), Google's codelab "Hands-on with Antigravity CLI" (1.1.27, https://codelabs.developers.google.com/antigravity-cli-hands-on), and the same words in the program text of the installed 1.5.9: "Accessing workspace:", the folder, "Do you trust the contents of this project?", "Antigravity CLI requires permission to read, edit, and execute files here.", "> Yes, I trust this folder" above "No, exit", "↑/↓ Navigate · enter Confirm". Its own choice is the trusting one: Enter accepts, and a ticked folder is answered. **Found with the real 1.5.9 the same day**: it draws one more row straight under the hint, its model and effort at the right edge ("Gemini 4 Carbon · medium"), and a ticked folder was not answered because the screen was held to end at the hint. That one row is now allowed, for Antigravity only and by its shape, since its words change with the model: the row straight after the hint, the last thing on the screen, 40 characters at most, with more room before it than it is long, and with no ">", "❯", "›", "?" or list number in it. Anything else under the hint is still not the screen. The real pane's rows are a fixture in `crates/agents/src/foldertrust.rs`.
- **Codex.** Left out on 16 Nov 2026 because its screen had not been captured; read since 18 Nov 2026 (see "A pair's folder is trusted when the recipe made it, and Codex's question is read" below).

**What trusting a folder allows, per program, in the program's own documentation.**

- **Claude Code** (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/security, "Trust verification"; https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions#project-allow-rules-and-workspace-trust and "What runs before you trust a folder"): "Claude Code'll be able to read, edit, and execute files here." After the dialog is accepted the folder's own `.claude/settings.json` applies: its `permissions.allow` rules and `additionalDirectories` ("grant capability, so Claude Code applies them only after you accept the workspace trust dialog"), a `headersHelper` on a server in `.mcp.json`, hooks in a project subagent's frontmatter, a project plugin folder and marketplaces the repository names, and a tracked `.claude/settings.local.json`. **Trust is wider than the folder**: "In a repository, Claude Code keys the trust on the git repository root, so the trust covers the whole repository ... In a worktree, it uses the main checkout's root"; outside a repository it covers the folder and every folder under it; in the home folder it lasts for that run only. Servers in `.mcp.json` still have their own approval prompt, which spandeck does not answer.
- **Antigravity** (the codelab above; Google publishes no fuller statement that was found): "Antigravity CLI requires permission to read, edit, and execute files here." Its trust is by exact path, kept as `trustedWorkspaces` in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json`; a subfolder is asked about again (seen by the owner).
- **Codex**, for the record (https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/config-file/config-reference, where developers.openai.com/codex/config-reference now leads; `projects.<path>.trust_level`): "Untrusted projects skip project-scoped `.codex/` layers, including project-local config, hooks, and rules."

**Decided by default on 16 Nov 2026** (the owner may overrule each, with the switch named):

- **The tick is off for a new folder, and a project's switch is off.** Nothing is trusted that the owner did not say.
- ~~Claude Code's marker is not moved for the owner.~~ **Overruled by the owner the same day** ("Fully automatic" below): `folder_trust.move` is on unless set to `false` (`[flags]` in the config, or `station.flags`). On, for an exact screen of a chosen folder whose marker is on "No, exit", spandeck sends the one arrow key that moves it, waits up to three seconds to read the marker on "Yes, I trust this folder", and only then presses Enter. It was first built off because Claude Code put its marker on "No" on purpose.
- **Unticking forgets the folder.** The list always says what the last answer was.
- **`folder_trust = false`** switches the whole of it off on a station: nothing is pressed on a standing choice; the one tap still works, being the owner's own.

**Fully automatic for a ticked folder (the same day, the owner's decision).** "Make spandeck fully automatic with codex and Claude or any agent". This answers what was left open above: **`folder_trust.move` is now on unless the owner turns it off.** For a folder the owner ticked (or a project they switched on), Claude Code's screen, whose marker starts on "No, exit", is answered by spandeck itself: the one arrow key that moves the marker, up to three seconds to read it on "Yes, I trust this folder" on a screen that is still the known one, and only then Enter; if the marker is not seen there nothing more is pressed and the pane offers the tap. `folder_trust.move = false` (`[flags]`, or `station.flags`) is the way back: then nothing is pressed for Claude Code, the dialog says so, and the pane offers the one tap. **Not changed, and the owner may overrule it: the tick itself is still off for a folder never used.** "Automatic" removes the extra tap for consent already given; trusting a folder nobody has looked at is a different risk. **Codex's folder question is still not answered**: the real Codex 0.169.0, started on 16 Nov 2026 in a new empty folder outside any repository, asked nothing, so there is no screen to build against.

**Mixed versions.** A Control Deck from before this has no tick and no button: its `pane.spawn` sends no tick, so a folder the owner chose earlier from a newer deck is still answered, and a pane that waits shows the words in `needs_reason` on its tile and its dot. A station from before this passes over `trust_folder` in `pane.spawn` and answers nothing; a deck shows the tick only where the station answers `foldertrust.choice`, and the lists only for a station that says `folder_trust` in `caps`. A station taken back to an older version keeps `folder_trust` in its store unread and passes over the three new fields of a pane's record.

**Not done here, and said so.** The scenario tests use the stand-in agent drawing each captured screen, on Linux. Run by hand against the real programs on 16 Nov 2026, on a throwaway station: Claude Code 2.1.325 not ticked (left alone, the tap offered), ticked (nothing pressed, the tap moved the marker and it started), and ticked with `folder_trust.move` (answered); Antigravity 1.5.9 ticked was not answered, for the status row above, and has not been run again since that was allowed for. A pane that first installs its program takes longer than 20 seconds to reach the question and is left to the owner. On Windows a pane's screen is read the same way but nothing was run there.

### A pair's folder is trusted when the recipe made it, and Codex's question is read (18 Nov 2026, the owner's request)

"For tests like this, spandeck should auto approve folder access." The second builder-and-critic run sat for two and a half hours at Claude Code's question, in a folder spandeck itself had made minutes before. `crates/core/src/recipes/pair.rs`, `crates/agents/src/foldertrust.rs`.

- **A recipe turns the project's switch on for a folder it makes.** `project new --recipe pair` on a folder that is not there, or is empty, makes it, makes it a repository and writes its files; nothing else is in it. The owner's command to make it is the consent, as starting a pane with the tick is: the project's switch ("Agents may be started in this project's folders without asking me to trust them") is turned on, the command says so, and the log has `foldertrust.project`. `--no-trust-folder` leaves the question to the owner. This overrules "a project's switch is off" for that case only.
- **Not for a folder that was there**, whatever is in it: each agent asks, and the command says so. `--trust-folder` is the owner's word for such a folder. A switch that is already on is left on.
- **Not for a new folder inside another git repository**: Claude Code keeps its trust for the whole repository, which would be more than the folder that was made. It asks, and the command says why.
- **Everything else in The folder-trust question holds**: the screen word for word for the pane's own folder, within 20 seconds of the start, once, the owner not having typed, and never a screen that says more.
- **Codex's screen** was read from a real 0.169.0 pane on spark2 (18 Nov 2026), started in a new git repository and closed unanswered: "Folder access", the folder, "Trust this folder? Codex can read, edit, and run files here, subject to your permission settings. Folder settings can run code automatically, even without a model request. Continue only if you trust these files. Your trust decision will be saved.", "› 1. Trust and continue" above "2. Back to Agent Command Center", "enter continue · esc back". Its own choice is the trusting one, so Enter accepts. The rows are a fixture in `crates/agents/src/foldertrust.rs`. Codex now has the tick box, the one tap and its line in Agent setup, like the other two. In a folder that was no git repository it did not ask.
- **Not covered.** When a newer Codex is out, Codex shows "Update available" before the question. spandeck does not answer that (it is neither a folder's trust nor spandeck's to decide), and once the owner has answered it they have typed into the pane, so the folder question that follows waits for the owner's one tap. Seen on spark2 with 0.169.0 when 0.170.0 was out. Codex's screen at other versions is not known; a screen that differs is left to the owner, as for every program.

## Trusting spandeck's own hooks (16 Nov 2026, the owner's decision)

Codex shows "Hooks need review" the first time on each machine, and again whenever spandeck's hooks change, and runs no hook until someone answers. The owner asked: "There is no way for spandeck to auto approve?" It was proposed that spandeck answer that screen only when every hook is its own. The owner: "When starting a new pane, would it be a checkmark for any agent to trust spandeck hooks? Should be checked on by default". `crates/agents/src/hookstrust.rs` (reads the screen and the files Codex takes hooks from, presses nothing), `crates/core/src/hookstrust.rs` (whose choice, the safety condition, the keys), and `crates/core/src/startscreen.rs`, which now holds what this and the folder-trust question have in common, once. No migration is added. This is the **second named exception** to "spandeck answers none of an agent program's own prompts"; the Guarantee line under Enforcement and `SECURITY.md` name both.

**The rule.** The owner's standing choice, visible where a pane is started and on by default, is the consent. spandeck then chooses "2. Trust all and continue" on that one screen, once per start of the program, and only when the hooks under review can only be spandeck's own.

- **Where the choice is made.** A tick in the New pane dialog and in the phone's New session sheet, "Trust spandeck's own hooks in <agent> if it asks", shown for a program that has such a review and whose screen spandeck can read (today Codex; another program is one screen and one list of hook sources in `agents/hookstrust.rs` away). `spandeck pane new --no-trust-hooks` is the tick taken off (`--trust-hooks` puts it back). Agent setup has the same choice as a switch per station, "Trust spandeck's own hooks when an agent asks". **On by default.** Unticking is remembered for the station and for later panes; ticking turns it on again.
- **Where it lives.** In the station's own key-value store (`hooks_trust`), like folder trust: **never in a project's file**, which a cloned repository could carry.
- **Who may use it (rule 7).** `pane.spawn` with `trust_hooks`, `hookstrust.choice`, `hookstrust.set` and `pane.trust_hooks` are the owner's: on no agent's list, no station's and not the read-only one; they reach another station only as the owner's forwarded request. The choice on a pane is worked out by whoever starts it and is never read from a caller's parameters. A pane an agent asks for (`delegate`) and a project's launcher start with the owner's standing choice as it is; an agent can neither set it nor call anything that answers the screen.
- **The safety condition: spandeck trusts only its own hooks.** "Trust all" trusts every new or changed hook Codex found, from every source. So before any key, and again each time the screen is looked at, the station reads the files Codex takes hooks from and presses nothing unless all of this holds:
  - the station is the one that keeps the agents' settings (`agent_home`, or the station of the account); another station installs no hooks and answers nothing;
  - the hooks file it manages (`~/.codex/hooks.json`) holds spandeck's entries and nothing else, and its `hooks` are **exactly** what this version writes for this station's own program path (the marker comment alone is something anyone could write);
  - there is no other source: no table of hooks in `~/.codex/config.toml` (anywhere in it, a profile's included; Codex's own `[hooks.state."…"]` trust records are not hooks), no `.codex/hooks.json` and no `.codex/config.toml` with hooks in the pane's folder or any folder above it, none in `/etc/codex` (`hooks.json`, `config.toml`, `managed_config.toml`, `requirements.toml`), no plugin switched on in any of those config files (a plugin can bring hooks, and which do cannot be read, so any plugin that is on counts), no hook setting on the program's own command line, and no other settings folder (`CODEX_HOME`); a file that cannot be read or parsed counts as a source;
  - the number on the screen is **at most** the number of spandeck's hooks (12 today). It is not held to equal the number not yet trusted: that would need Codex's `trusted_hash` of each hook, and how it is made is not documented, so it is not guessed.
  - On Windows nothing is answered: where Codex keeps the machine's own settings there is not known.
- **A refusal lasts for that start of the program.** Codex reads its hooks when it starts. If the files did not show only spandeck's hooks when the pane was first looked at, and are put right while it waits, the screen still asks about what Codex found then: that pane stays refused until its program is started again.
- **What is answered** is held to everything the folder-trust question is: the program's own screen **from its first row to its last** (below), first seen within 20 seconds of the program's start and before any hook of the session, unchanged for 0.7 seconds, the owner not having typed into the pane, the choice still on when the key would be pressed, once per start of the program. The marker starts on "1. Review hooks". Moving it is part of what the owner chose, so there is no extra switch: Down is sent, the screen must stand again, exact, with the marker **seen on "2. Trust all and continue"**, the files are read again, and only then Enter. If the marker has not moved within three seconds nothing more is pressed. The keys are items of the pane's input (`terminal/input.rs`); how the choice is reached is one small function (`agents/hookstrust.rs`, `step`).
- **Codex's own trust records are never written** (`[hooks.state]` in `~/.codex/config.toml`): keys are pressed, and the program records what it records.
- **When nothing is pressed** the pane says why in its `needs_reason`, and `hooks_trust.asks` in its view has `why` and `button`:
  - the hooks may not all be spandeck's (`not_only_spandecks`, no button, the owner reviews in the terminal): "asks you to trust hooks: 3 hooks in its hooks file are not spandeck's. Review them in the terminal", "…spandeck's hooks in its hooks file are not the ones this version installs (run `spandeck integration install codex`)", "…spandeck's hooks are not installed for it here", "…another hooks file is in this project or on this machine (<path>)", "…<path> has hooks of its own", "…<path> switches a plugin on, and a plugin can bring hooks", "…<path> could not be read", "…it was started with another settings folder (CODEX_HOME)", "…it was started with hook settings on its command line", "…it asks about 13, and spandeck has 12", "…this station does not keep spandeck's hooks (…)", "…on this system spandeck cannot tell whose they are", "…the hooks it found were not only spandeck's when it started, whatever the files say now (start the pane again to have it look afresh)";
  - they are spandeck's own and nothing was pressed all the same: "asks you to trust spandeck's hooks: answer in the terminal", with `why` `not_chosen` (the choice is off, or the pane was started without it), `you_typed`, `not_the_known_screen` (reworded, a row more, a narrower terminal) or `marker_did_not_move`. Where the three choices and the count can be read the deck offers **Trust spandeck's hooks** (`pane.trust_hooks`), the owner's own tap: it checks the same safety condition, moves the marker, reads the screen again and confirms only with the marker seen on the trusting choice.
  - A review first seen later than 20 seconds after the start is not read here at all; the pane says "asks you to review hooks: answer in the terminal (run /hooks)" from the ordinary screen rules.
- **On record.** `pane.hooks_trusted` in the ship's log (the pane, the program, `how`: `standing` or `tap`, who started the pane); `hookstrust.set` records the choice. The pane says "spandeck answered Codex's review of hooks with "Trust all and continue": every hook was spandeck's own" for ten minutes. No notice is raised for a review spandeck answered.
- **Restart.** A program started again in the same pane (Restart, an update, the station after a crash) is treated the same way, and only for a pane that was started with the choice, which is on the pane's record. Answering lifts no Pause and no hold; the owner's own tap counts as the owner typing, which releases a hold and never ends a Pause.
- **Said elsewhere.** `spandeck integration install codex` and `spandeck doctor` say that the review is answered when the choice is on, and the doctor says whether it is on at this station.

**The screen, and where it comes from.** Codex 0.169.0, read from a real pane by the owner on 16 Nov 2026 (the rows that were not empty): "Hooks need review", "12 hooks are new or changed.", "Hooks can run outside the sandbox after you trust them.", "› 1. Review hooks", "2. Trust all and continue", "3. Continue without trusting (hooks won't run)", "enter confirm · esc skip". The sentence for one hook, "1 hook is new or changed.", is from the words in 0.169.0's program text (noted in this repository before the capture) and has not been seen on a screen. **Not known, and so not allowed for**: what a taller screen has above the title (anything there makes it "not the known screen": the tap, no automatic answer), whether a digit key selects, and whether Codex shows its folder question before this review (a key the owner pressed for that question makes this one theirs too).

**Decided by default on 16 Nov 2026** (the owner may overrule each):

- **On when the caller says nothing**, unlike folder trust, which is off for a folder never used. That is acceptable here because what is trusted is spandeck's own hooks, which the owner installed with `spandeck integration install codex`, and never anything else: the safety condition is what bounds it, not the tick.
- **Any plugin that is on stops it**, and so does a project's `.codex/hooks.json` even in a project Codex would not load it from. Both lean towards asking the owner.
- **The count is "at most"**, not "equal" (above).
- **No station flag**: the standing choice is the one switch.

**Mixed versions.** A Control Deck from before this has no tick: its `pane.spawn` sends none, so the standing choice (on unless turned off from a newer deck or the command line) is used, and a pane that waits shows its reason on its tile. A station from before this passes over `trust_hooks` and answers nothing; a deck shows the tick only where the station answers `hookstrust.choice`, and the switch in Agent setup only for a station that says `hooks_trust` in `caps`. A station taken back to an older version keeps `hooks_trust` in its store unread and passes over the three new fields of a pane's record (`hooks_trust_choice`, `hooks_trust_answered`, `hooks_trust_asks`); a pane's record written before this has none and is not answered until its pane is started anew.

**Not done here, and said so.** The scenario tests use the stand-in agent drawing the captured screen, on Linux, with Codex's settings in a throwaway home. Nothing was run against the real Codex: the keys (Down, then Enter) follow the screen's own hint and are unproven. In the conversation face of a pane the review shows as the ordinary "Waiting in the terminal" card with the reason; the one tap is on the pane's strip, its menu and the phone's terminal face.

## AgentVault (14 Nov 2026)

- spandeck and AgentVault stay separate programs. spandeck works without a vault.
- The vault is the only place a token is issued, and only when the owner approves on the vault's own page. The vault passphrase is never typed into the Control Deck. Approval opens in a small pop-up window on the vault's address.
- One token per station, held by spandeckd in a file only the user can read. Panes and agents never get the token, and keys are never put in a pane's environment.
- The token can list keys and send requests through the vault's proxy, read-only by default, and is pinned to the station's Tailscale node.
- spandeck finds the vault by its Tailscale name; the address is never hard-coded. `[vault] url` in the config overrides it.
- Tokens do not expire by default. Rotation is a per-station setting (never, or every N hours, at least 1) that only the owner changes; spandeckd rotates by itself.
- The owner is told when the setting changes outside the Control Deck, or the token is swapped or refused.
- Connecting a station is the only step: by default its agents may use every key the owner allowed that station's token at the vault (changed 14 Nov 2026 at the owner's request; before, a project had to list its keys). A project can narrow its agents with `[vault] keys` in `.spandeck/project.toml`.
- When a station has a token, a raw key in an agent's command is blocked and reported to the vault; one pasted into a prompt, or found in a transcript, is reported.
- **Connect prompt** (the owner's request, 14 Nov 2026: "there should be nothing I need to do"). A Control Deck that opens on a station that found a vault and has no working token says so once, in a bar that blocks nothing, with one button that starts the connect flow. "Not now" is kept by the station (so it holds in every browser), is undone from the Vault screen, and is forgotten when the station connects. No prompt when no vault was found or `[vault] enabled = false`.
- **Check and repair.** spandeckd checks the chain from an agent to a key when it starts, when a Control Deck opens (at most once a minute) and when asked (`vault.check`, the owner's only): the vault's address, its lock, the station's token (accepted, and tied to a machine), the key list, and what panes need (spandeck's own program and socket; Antigravity's plugin). It repairs without the owner: a token the vault calls overdue is swapped, Antigravity's plugin is rewritten (since 16 Nov 2026 by the station of the account only: see Only the station of the account writes the agents' settings in your home). Each thing it cannot repair is one sentence with one action, in `vault.status` (`checks`, `health`), on the Vault screen and in `spandeck doctor`, and is told to the owner once (`vault.alert`), not on every check and not again after a restart.
- A repair never gets a token any way but the owner's approval at the vault.
- A connected station shows its token to no address but the one that issued it (a security fix of 14 Nov 2026: no other machine can ask for the token by claiming to be a vault). When that address stops answering and another AgentVault answers on the owner's machines, the station does not try its token there: it says "The vault seems to have moved to <address>" and offers Reconnect, the ordinary approval. Following a move with the existing token is behind `[vault] follow_moves = true`, off by default: relaxing the guard is the owner's decision, and the owner has not made it.

## Agent programs (14 Nov 2026, the owner's request)

- OpenCode and Antigravity are agents a pane can run, wired to spandeck's tools and skill when the pane starts. Neither can block a tool call or pass an approval to the Control Deck, so their rules are guidance. (Antigravity's wiring is a plugin in the home folder, which since 16 Nov 2026 only the station of the account writes: see Only the station of the account writes the agents' settings in your home.)
- A pane started for an agent that is not installed on its station installs it first, in the pane, with the vendor's own install command (or the way the owner chose for that station, see below). That command is fixed in the code (a vendor's script is fetched over HTTPS only) or set in the station's own config; it never comes from a caller or another station.
- Looking for an agent's program has four answers, never fewer (14 Nov 2026, after a fault on the owner's Mac: a Codex whose real binary was gone was called installed, and one that was slow to answer was called not found): not installed; installed (it answered when asked its version); installed but not working (it exits with an error or cannot be run); installed but it did not answer in time, which is unknown, not broken. A program that is slow to say its version is never killed.
- When a program is in several folders spandeck looks in, the first copy that answers is used, not the first that exists.
- A pane started for a program that is installed but not working offers a reinstall in the pane, the same way a missing one is installed: what was found and the program's own error line, the command, Ctrl-C skips. Success is the program answering afterwards, not the installer's exit code. Whether an install should ask before it runs is still open with the owner; for now it announces the command and Ctrl-C skips.
- Each agent has the ways it can be installed that its vendor documents: the vendor's own script (the default), npm, Homebrew. A way is offered on a station only when its tool is there. The owner's choice is kept per station and per agent in the station's state; `install_cmd` in the station's config still wins over everything. Every command is fixed in the code, taken from the vendor's documentation or a package registry; a way that could not be confirmed is left out. Installing, like updating, is the owner's to do (`agents.install`): an agent cannot.
- The owner can see each agent's version on each station and update one or all of them. After an update that changed the version, an idle pane of that agent restarts into its conversation; a pane in a turn, or waiting on the owner, restarts when it is next idle. Updating is the owner's to do: an agent cannot.

### Every action says what it did, one job at a time, and is this the latest? (17 Nov 2026, the owner's report)

The owner, from the Agent programs dialog on an iPhone: "I picked update all on m4-MacBook, no feedback, don't know if it's doing it, if it worked, don't know if the version listed is the latest or not". Earlier the same day: "How do I know if an agent is up to date? when I start an agent does it ensure it's the latest version? Does it run an update check. Or is there an update command somewhere?" And: "We should also ensure that I can't press update twice in a row, it should grey out until the update process is complete". On that fleet the press for M4-Macbook left no entry in that station's log at all, and another station's log had two updates of one program six seconds apart.

- **An update or an install is a job its station keeps**, one per program (the newest): what was asked and by whom, when it started, what is run, the last line it has printed, and how it ended. `agents.status` carries it on the program's row, so the dialog shows the same on every device, after it is closed and opened again, and whether or not it was open when the job started. The dialog asks each station by itself: a slow one holds up only its own rows.
- **How it ended, in these words**: "Updated 2.1.326 → 2.1.330"; "Already the latest: 2.1.326" (the updater ran and the version is what it was: for Claude Code that is the updater's own answer, "Claude Code is up to date"); "Could not update: " and the program's own error line, with all it printed behind "Show what it said"; "Not known whether it finished: interrupted by a restart of spandeck on …". Update all shows "Updating 4 programs on M4-Macbook… 2 of 4" under the station's name, then "2 updated, 2 already the latest" or "1 failed: Codex", and each row its own result. A result stays on its row for ten minutes or while the dialog that saw it is open; a failure stays until something else is done. What a program printed is text, with escape sequences and control characters taken out, and its end is kept (6,000 characters).
- **One job at a time per station and program.** A second `agents.update` or `agents.install` for a program whose job is running starts nothing: it is answered with the running job (its id, when it started, who asked). Update all skips the programs that are running and says so. In the deck Update, Reinstall and Install on a row are greyed (`disabled`, `aria-disabled`) from the press until the station says that row's job has ended, and so are the station's "Update all here" and the fleet's "Update all" while any of their rows is running; rows of other stations stay usable. The greying comes from the station's record, not the page's memory.
- **A job that hangs does not grey a row for ever.** An updater or installer is given 15 minutes (`[flags] agents.job_limit_secs`): generous, since an installer downloads a few hundred megabytes. After that it is stopped and the job ends as failed, with what it had printed. Only the command spandeck started is stopped; something it started in turn may run on.
- **A restart ends a job honestly.** The record is stored when the job starts and when it ends. A job that was running when spandeck stopped is, at the next start, "interrupted by a restart": whether the command finished is not known, and the version shown is what the program says now.
- **`station.update` does not wait for a slow download (21 Nov 2026, a bug that happened twice).** `spandeck update --station rtx6000-wsl` said "did not answer within 5 s" and the station was never updated: a release is about 100 MB, the answer waited for the whole download, and the download ended with the call that stopped waiting. The download is now the station's own work. It is waited for 1.5 seconds, so a quick one is answered as before (`from`, `release`); a slow one is answered `{ok, begun: true, from, release, note}`, goes on by itself, and one notice says how it went ("alpha is downloading v…", then that it is updating, or why it was not). Asked again meanwhile: refused, "already getting a release". A download that fails after the answer goes on to the fleet as before. Test: `crates/cli/tests/supply.rs`, a release host that takes seven seconds. **And a slow line is expected** (the owner, the same day: "some machines will be slow to download, and we should support that"): the archive is given as long as it takes while it keeps coming, and is given up only when nothing arrives for a minute (before: five minutes in all, whatever the line). **What is published is the production build** (the same day): no debug information (`debug = 0`, `strip = "debuginfo"`; function names are kept), and `sd` is packed as a hard link to `spandeck`. A release for Linux went from 110 MB to about 17 MB. Not seen on a published release yet: the workflow's packing is tried by the next tag.
- **`agents.update` and `agents.install` answer at once and wait for nothing.** Before, `agents.update` read each program's version first, up to five seconds each, and `agents.status` read them one after another; a call from another station is given five seconds, so a station whose programs were slow to answer lost its whole answer, and the page was told "could not confirm whether this was carried out" in a notice that was gone in seven seconds, while the update ran or did not. Now the job reads the version itself, `agents.status` reads the versions side by side and waits three seconds at most (a program still being asked is shown as last seen, and marked), and whatever the page is told goes on the row: "Not started: " and the station's reason, "Could not start: " and its words, or that the station did not confirm in time and nothing is running there. Why the press for M4-Macbook left nothing in its log could not be established from the code alone: no path was found in which the request arrives and runs nothing without an entry, so it did not arrive or was given up before it was read (see the report with this change for the candidates and what to look at on that machine).
- **The rule for programs with running panes is unchanged**, and the row says it: after an update that changed the version, an idle pane of that program restarts into its conversation; a pane in a turn, or waiting on the owner, restarts when its turn is over ("1 running: it restarts after an update"; the result says how many did). A reinstall restarts nothing. `agents.update` takes `restart: false` (`spandeck agents update --no-restart`) to leave running panes as they are.
- **Is it the latest?** Each station asks, for each program it has, the place that program's own installer, or the package manager the copy was installed with, takes its newest version from (`crates/agents/src/catalogue.rs`, each with its source and the date it was read, 17 Nov 2026): Claude Code `downloads.claude.ai/claude-code-releases/latest` (what `claude.ai/install.sh` reads); Codex `releases.openai.com/codex/channels/latest` (what its install script reads first); OpenCode `opencode.ai/update/api/latest/cli/npm` (what its v2 install script reads; GitHub's "latest release" of OpenCode is version 1); Antigravity the manifest its install script reads; a copy installed with npm, `npm view <package> version`; with Homebrew, `brew info --json=v2`. The way a copy was installed is the way the owner chose on that station, else what its path shows (`node_modules`, Homebrew's Cellar or Caskroom), else the vendor's script. No program has a documented "check only" flag, so none is run to find out. Beside the version the row says "✓ latest", "2.1.330 available" (and Update stands out), or "could not check: " and why ("no route to downloads.claude.ai"); a line at the top says "Everything is the latest" or "3 updates available on 2 stations", with Update all.
- **When it is asked.** When the owner asks: as the dialog opens, `spandeck agents`, or "Check again". An answer is kept three hours (a failure one minute) and reused; nothing is asked in the background. **Starting an agent never checks for an update and never installs one**: an update under running sessions can break them, a pane's start must not wait on a release host, and what is running is the owner's to decide. What each program does by itself, from its vendor's pages (17 Nov 2026), is shown at the foot of the dialog: Claude Code installed with Anthropic's script or npm updates itself in the background, the new version taking effect at its next start, and one from Homebrew or WinGet does not; Codex checks at start (`check_for_update_on_startup`), and whether it installs without being asked was not established; OpenCode tells you and installs only if its config says `update = "auto"`; Antigravity updates itself in the background.
- **What a check tells an outside party**: that a machine at this address asked for a public version number. Nothing of the owner, the station or a project is sent. `[agents] check_latest = false` in a station's config turns it off there: nothing is asked, and the dialog says checks are off. The calls go through the same HTTP client as every other outside call (`http.rs`), with the system's proxy settings, three seconds to connect and eight for the answer.
- **The same from the command line**: `spandeck agents` lists, per station, each program, its version, latest or available, and its last result; `spandeck agents update [AGENT] [--station X | --all] [--no-restart]` shows the same progress and outcome, and exits with an error if any failed.
- **Every card has one layout**: the name, version and marks; how it is installed; the actions at the right, in one order (Reinstall, then Update; Install alone when it is not installed), 44 px high on a phone. Station names stay in sight while their cards are scrolled.
- **Mixed versions.** A station from before (0.4.11) has no jobs and cannot say what is the latest: under its name the dialog says so, its rows show version and health as before, and Update there still works, greyed while that station says it is updating, with "updated 2m ago" or "the last update failed" from its log afterwards. `station.info` says `agent_jobs` in `caps` where a station has all this.
- Starting an update, starting an install and making a station ask a release host are the owner's alone (rule 7): no agent, no read-only caller and no other station on its own account may call `agents.update`, `agents.install` or `agents.check`.

## Tools for agents (18 Nov 2026, the owner's request)

The owner: "I wonder if we should have a mcp or tool section for spandeck. One where we add a mcp or tool and it automatically do the setup on every machine and for every agent… it then would ask for this machine or the fleet? … I can come back later and decide to roll out across the fleet … It also lets me upgrade across the fleet". To the plan's questions: "Agree with your recommendation" (spandeck keeps its own runtimes; a tool for the fleet installs on a station that joins later; a tool names an AgentVault key; a "without asking" tick per tool), and "Build out the fleet mcp plan".

What was there before: Agent setup's list of MCP servers. It is a line of configuration for the whole fleet. It installs nothing, has no "this station only" and no version, and hands every agent a command whether or not the command exists on the station.

**Built: the station side.** The calls (`docs/tools-api.md`), the command line (`spandeck tools`), and what a pane is given. **And the Control Deck's screen** (the Tools dialog, below; Agent setup's "MCP servers" part is as it was, with a line that points to Tools). **Not built:** keys through AgentVault (below), and the pair recipe's use of the browser tool.

- **A tool is a catalogue entry or a custom one.** A catalogue entry is fixed in the code (`crates/core/src/toolcat.rs`): the package, the commands, the arguments an agent starts it with, where its newest version is published, each with where it was read and when. A caller names an entry and a version, never a command or an address. A custom tool is a command or an address of the owner's, exactly what an MCP server of Agent setup is: spandeck installs nothing for it and only looks whether the command is on the station. Playwright (`@playwright/mcp`) is the first entry and the only one.
- **The choice is one document every station keeps, `tools.json`** (`crates/core/src/tooldoc.rs`), by the rules of the other synced documents (The same view from every station): which tools, for which agents, for the whole fleet or the stations named, the version the fleet runs, the stations that run another one, the "without asking" tick. "The whole fleet" includes a station that joins later.
- **It is a document of its own, not a field of the agent setup, because of the stations on 0.4.15.** Read from the code of 0.4.15 (`setup.rs`, `sync.rs`): a station reads the agent setup into the fields it knows and writes those back, so a field it does not know is gone from its copy; its copy carries the same stamp as the copy it was made from, so no station would ever see a difference; and a station that is behind takes a newer copy from whichever station it looks at first. A tool kept in the agent setup would therefore be missing from every 0.4.15 station's copy, and from every newer station that happened to take its copy from one of them, with nothing to say so. The look and preferences documents were made documents of their own for the same reason.
  - **What a 0.4.15 station does with the new document: nothing.** It is never offered it (it says nothing of it in `station.info`), is never asked for it, and its own documents are not changed by it. It cannot destroy it and does not pass it on; stations that keep it pass it among themselves directly.
  - **The limits.** A 0.4.15 station installs no tool and gives its agents no tool of the new document, catalogue or custom: a deck shows "needs a newer spandeck" for it (`tools` in `caps`). It goes on giving its agents the MCP servers of Agent setup, which are untouched. An MCP server of Agent setup that the owner gives a scope or the "without asking" tick is moved into the new document (written there first, then taken out of the agent setup): from then a 0.4.15 station no longer gives it to its agents. A later format of the document is not taken by a station that does not read it (`format`), as with preferences.
- **The MCP servers of Agent setup work as before.** They are listed as custom tools for the whole fleet (`from_setup`), and are given to every agent on every station, found or not. A custom tool of the new document is given the same way wherever it is chosen: whether its command is on the station is reported (`needs`: "there is no `npx` on spark2") and does not stop it being given, because that is what happens today and the owner's existing entries must not change. A tool may not take the name of an MCP server; if both exist all the same, the tool is the one given.
- **Each station acts for itself** (`crates/core/src/tools.rs`). When it starts, and when its copy of the document changes, a station installs each catalogue tool that is for it and not there at the version pinned for it. No station installs on another, and none waits for another. An install is a job the station keeps, one at a time per tool, in the shape and the words of an agent program's job: "Installed 0.0.85", "Upgraded 0.0.84 → 0.0.85", "Rolled back 0.0.85 → 0.0.84", "Could not install: " and the program's own line, with what was printed behind it. It is given 15 minutes. One cut short by a restart is ended as interrupted and run again at the start.
  - **An install that failed is not tried again by the station.** The same failure and the same downloads, again at every start and every change, help nobody. The owner retries it (`tools.install`, Retry), or changes the version. Decided by default; the owner may overrule.
  - **No job holds up anything.** A job runs on a thread of its own. Starting a pane, a deck's snapshot and another station's call read the disk and return.
- **spandeck brings its own runtime** (the owner's decision: no dependence on what the machine has, no administrator). A tool that needs Node gets Node 24.22.3, downloaded once from `https://nodejs.org/dist/v24.22.3/` over HTTPS through spandeck's own client into `<state>/tools/runtime/node-24.22.3/`. The version and the SHA-256 of each platform's archive are fixed in the code (from that version's `SHASUMS256.txt`, read 18 Nov 2026: linux-arm64, linux-x64, darwin-arm64, darwin-x64 as `.tar.gz`, win-x64 and win-arm64 as `.zip`); an archive that does not match is refused and never unpacked. It is unpacked with `tar`: no pure-Rust way to read a tar archive is among the dependencies (`flate2` is, for gzip only), `tar` is on Linux, macOS and Windows 10 and later (where it reads `.zip` too), and it is what a release of spandeck is already unpacked with. npm is started as `node …/npm-cli.js`, because npm's own launcher looks for a `node` on the path.
- **How a tool is installed.** Into `<state>/tools/<name>/<version>/` with that Node's npm: `npm install --prefix <folder> --cache <state>/tools/runtime/npm-cache --ignore-scripts @playwright/mcp@<version>`. Nothing is installed globally, nothing is written outside spandeck's state folder, and no install script is run (the package and what it depends on have none). Then its browser: `node node_modules/playwright/cli.js install chromium`, with `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` set to `<state>/tools/<name>/data/`, so that it is shared by the tool's versions and not with whatever the machine has. Then, **as the last step, the browser is started once, headless**, and closed. Only then is the version marked ready. If that last step fails, the tool is not ready and its state is "needs something this station lacks", with the program's own line (for example a missing system library).
- **An agent is given a catalogue tool only where it is ready.** Every place a pane's servers are written (Claude Code's `--mcp-config`, Codex's `-c mcp_servers.…`, OpenCode's `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`, Antigravity's plugin, which is written again when a tool becomes ready, by the station of the account only) gets the tool as: spandeck's Node by its whole path, the tool's entry file by its whole path, `--headless --isolated --browser chromium`, and `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`. A pane started while a tool is installing, or after its install failed, starts without it, and its record says so (`pane.created`: `tools_without`, with why).
  - **`--browser chromium`**, not the default `chrome`: `chromium` is the browser Playwright downloads itself; `chrome` is Google Chrome as installed on the machine, which is not spandeck's to install and does not exist for Linux on arm64.
  - **While an upgrade installs, or after one failed, agents keep the version that worked**, as long as it is on the disk. The station's row says which version is pinned and which is given, so the difference shows.
- **Versions.** The document pins a version for the fleet; a station can have one of its own. `tools.upgrade` sets one station's; `tools.rollout` sets the fleet's and takes the stations' own away; going back is choosing the earlier version. A station keeps the version it ran before the current one and removes older ones once the new one is ready, so going back one is at once. After a change of version on a station, the panes of the agents the tool is for are treated as after an agent program's update: an idle one restarts into its conversation, one in a turn or waiting on the owner when its turn is over; `restart: false` leaves them. A first install restarts nothing: it takes effect in panes started from now.
- **Is there a newer one?** Asked of the registry (`registry.npmjs.org/@playwright/mcp/latest`) only when the owner asks (`tools.check`, `spandeck tools`, and when a tool is added or upgraded with no version named), kept three hours, and never with `[agents] check_latest = false`: exactly as for agent programs. With no answer, a tool is added at the version spandeck was run with (0.0.85), and the answer says so.
- **When a tool is removed or leaves a station's scope, it is no longer given to panes started from then, and its files stay** under `<state>/tools/<name>/`. Narrowing a scope by mistake should not cost the download again. On this machine one installed version was 19 MB, its browsers 694 MB and the runtime with npm's cache 228 MB. Nothing removes them but the owner; a call that does was not built.
- **"Agents may use this without asking me"** is off unless ticked, and is written only where a program has a rule for one server's tools that spandeck already uses for its own server: Claude Code (`mcp__<name>` added to the pane's `--allowedTools`) and Codex (`mcp_servers.<name>.default_tools_approval_mode="approve"`). Never a rule over all servers. **Nothing is written for OpenCode** (spandeck writes no such rule for it today) **or for Antigravity**: its rules are in the owner's own settings file, which spandeck changes only on the owner's `integration install`, with four named tools and never a whole server (Every agent can talk to every other agent, 8). That stands; the tick does nothing there, and the API's document says to say so beside it.
- **Every `tools.` call is the owner's alone** (rule 7): on no list of an agent's, a read-only caller's or a station's. A station has one, `tools.push`, to offer its copy of the document. Another station is reached as the owner's forwarded request.
- **A station's own config can replace the install** (`[tools.<id>]`: `install_cmd`, `check_cmd`, `runtime`, `latest_url`), as `[agents.<name>]` does for an agent program: for tests, a mirror, or a station that must use a Node of its own. It is that station's own file.

**Found on this machine** (spark2: Linux, arm64, no Node, no administrator; 18 Nov 2026), by a station started for the purpose installing `playwright` 0.0.85 with no stand-in:

- The Node archive was 58,051,304 bytes and matched the SHA-256 in the code. `npm install --ignore-scripts` added 3 packages in 1 s.
- `playwright install chromium` downloaded Chrome for Testing 158.0.8127.18 (195.7 MiB), FFmpeg (1.9 MiB) and Chrome Headless Shell 158.0.8127.18 (123.9 MiB) from `cdn.playwright.dev`.
- The browser started headless and closed: "started Chromium 158.0.8127.18 headless and closed it". The whole job took 15 seconds on this machine's connection.
- The server, started exactly as a Claude Code pane is given it, answered `initialize` as "Playwright" 1.66.0-alpha-2026-11-09 and opened a page with `browser_navigate`.
- The registry's `latest` for `@playwright/mcp` was 0.0.85: no install scripts, `engines` node >=18, depending on playwright 1.66.0-alpha-2026-11-09.

**Not known.**

- **macOS and Windows.** Nothing was run there. The archive names and sums are from nodejs.org's list; that Windows' `tar` unpacks Node's `.zip` into place, and that the browser starts there without an administrator, are not established. The Windows build was type-checked only.
- **A Linux machine that lacks the browser's system libraries.** spark2 has them. What Playwright prints there, and so what the "needs" line reads like, was not seen; `playwright install-deps`, which installs system packages, is never run by spandeck.
- **Whether the two "without asking" rules work for this server.** Neither was run with a real Claude Code or Codex.
- **The server writes files into the folder it is started in** (`.playwright-mcp/`, a snapshot of each page): seen in the run above. In a pane that is the agent's working folder, which may be a repository. `--output-dir` would move them; whether agents need them where they are was not established, and nothing was set.
- **What the tool's definitions cost an agent's context.** Not measured. The per-agent choice exists.
- **Playwright's README** was not read here: "Node 18 or newer" is the registry's `engines`; the brief for this build said the README says 20. spandeck's Node is 24.

**Left for later.** Keys through AgentVault: a custom tool's `env` is still in the synced document, as an MCP server's is today; it waits on the vault's side (a station's token cannot read a value). Removing a tool's files. A second catalogue entry.

**Tests** (`crates/cli/tests/tools.rs`, with a stand-in installer and a registry on the machine; `toolcat.rs`; `rpc.rs`). A tool for one station is installed and given there only, and widening its scope makes the other station install it by itself; a pane started while it is not ready does not get it and its record says so, and one started after does; an upgrade of one station changes only that station, restarts its idle pane on the new version, a rollout brings the other, and the earlier version chosen again is honoured without installing; the tick writes the rule for that one server in Claude Code and Codex; an agent is refused every call; the grid and an upgrade from the command line. A failed install says the program's own line and is not tried again by itself, the owner's retry starts it and a second request while it runs starts nothing; a tool that does not start says what the station lacks and agents keep the version that works; the registry is asked when the owner asks and the answer is kept; an MCP server of Agent setup is given as before and listed; a job cut short by a restart is run again. A Node archive whose SHA-256 does not match is refused. No agent, read-only caller or station has a `tools.` call that changes anything. One test installs the real tool and is run by hand (it needs the internet).

**Mixed versions.** Above: a station from before has no `tools` in `caps`, is shown as needing a newer spandeck, and keeps giving its agents the MCP servers of Agent setup.

### The Tools dialog (18 Nov 2026)

The screen for the above (`crates/web/assets/tools.js`), built on `docs/tools-api.md` and nothing else. It is reached from the top bar ("Tools", beside Agent setup), the command palette, a station's menu (beside "Agent programs and versions…") and, on a phone, the top bar's menu. It follows the Agent programs dialog in everything "Every action says what it did, one job at a time" decides.

- **A card for each tool, and in it a row for each station**, at every width: the station's name, how the tool stands there in words ("not chosen here", "installing" with the job's last line, "ready 0.0.85", "failed" or "needs something" with the program's own line and "Show what it said", "needs a newer spandeck" for a station without `tools` in `caps`, "not answering"), then what its job is doing or how it ended in the station's own words (`says`), then the row's actions. Where there is room (wider than a phone) a small table above the cards gives the same tool by station at a glance. Each station is asked by itself (`tools.status`); which tools there are is the document of the station serving the page (`tools.list`). A row whose station has not yet taken the document that is shown says "Waiting for <station> to hear of the change".
- **Add a tool** is a dialog of its own: which tool (the catalogue's entries with what each gives and needs, or "A tool of your own": a name and a command or an address), then **"Where?"**: "This station (<the station the page is on>)" (the default), "Choose stations", or "The whole fleet, and stations that join later"; for which agents (all, unless unticked); and "Agents may use this without asking me" (off), which says under it that Claude Code and Codex honour it today. Adding closes the form; the grid shows the installs. A version that was not the registry's answer (`version_from: "catalogue"`) is said on the tool's card.
- **Later, on the tool's card**: "Roll out to the whole fleet" while its scope is narrower; "Change where" (the same "Where?", the agents and the tick; for an MCP server of Agent setup it says that the change makes it a tool of its own); "Remove", which asks once and says that agents lose the tool at their next start and that its files stay on disk.
- **Versions.** The card says the fleet's version, each station that runs another ("bravo runs 0.0.86"), and whether a newer one is published ("✓ latest", "0.0.86 available", "could not check: …"; "Check again" asks every station's registry anew). A row offers "Upgrade on <station>" when a newer one is published; once a station runs a version of its own and it is ready there, the card offers "Roll out <version> to the fleet"; a row offers "Go back to <version>" while an earlier one is on that station's disk. Each of the three asks first, says in the API's words what happens to running panes, and has a tick to leave them as they are (`restart: false`). Going back for the whole fleet is going back on one station and rolling that out.
- **Try again** on a failed row, or one that needs something (`tools.install` at that station).
- **Greyed from the press until the station's record says the job ended** (`disabled`, `aria-disabled`): a row's actions while that station's job runs or its copy of the document is behind the one shown, the tool's own actions while any of its rows is so. It comes from what the stations answer, so it is the same after the dialog is closed and opened again and on another device. While a row is busy its one button says what is being done ("Installing…", "Upgrading…", "Going back…"). A result stays on its row for ten minutes or while the dialog that saw it is open; one that did not end well stays.
- **`tools.updated` and `tools.status` mean "ask again"**: however many arrive, a station is asked one question at a time and at most four times a second; a station with a job running is asked every second and a half, the others every eight. A card is drawn again only when what it shows changed, and the other cards are left as they are, so the place scrolled to, an open "Show what it said" and the form being typed in are kept.
- **On a phone** the dialog and the form are sheets (the whole screen, the Close clear of the clock, a Close at the foot), every target is 44 px, and nothing is wider than the page at 320 px.
- **Not staged in a test**: a station without `tools` in `caps`. No flag turns the cap off and no older station can be started in a test, so "needs a newer spandeck" is drawn from `caps` and was not seen in a browser.

**Tests** (`crates/cli/tests/tools_deck.rs`, merge tier, a real browser, the stand-in installer of `tools.rs`): the owner's request from end to end on a phone (added for this station, ready here and not chosen there; rolled out, and the other is ready without reopening; a newer version found, upgraded on one station, rolled out, gone back; the scope narrowed again); a failed install with the program's line as text, what it printed kept open while the stations are asked again, "Try again" greyed from the press, through closing and reopening, until the station says the job ended, a station that does not answer, an MCP server of Agent setup listed, and Remove; at 320 px nothing wider than the page, a tool of the owner's own added through the form, and both dialogs closed by their own control.

## Add a machine (14 Nov 2026, the owner's request)

- The fleet screen ends in an "Add a machine" tile. It lists the Mac and Linux machines under the owner's Tailscale login that are online and not stations; phones, Windows, tagged and shared machines are left out. The station the owner is using connects to the one picked over SSH, runs the same install script as the README, starts spandeck there now and at login, and has it ask to join as `spandeck fleet --join` does. The tile then shows the join code.
- **The approval step stays.** The new station is approved with the usual control, by its code. A code that arrives over SSH is shown only if this station holds a waiting request with the same code. The join step is one place in the code (`JoinBy` in `crates/core/src/add.rs`), so a single-use ticket could replace the code later; no ticket exists.
- **Its own setting, off by default: `add_machines = true`** in the config of the station that does the adding, not `remote_start`. Both let a station run a command on another machine, and both are off until the owner turns them on at that station. They are separate because they differ in what they risk: `remote_start` runs one fixed command on a machine that is already a station, with access the owner already set up; adding installs software on a machine spandeck has never run on, and can use a password typed into the deck. An owner who wants one has not agreed to the other. The picker works while it is off and says how to turn it on; nothing is run and no password is accepted.
- The owner can also turn it on, and off again, from the tile itself (changed 14 Nov 2026: the owner clicked the tile and asked why they could not add a machine). The switch is kept in the station's state, is logged, is the owner's alone (`fleet.add_enable` is on no agent or station list and is never forwarded), and applies only to the station whose Control Deck they are using. It stays off until the owner turns it on.
- **Only the owner, and only at the station being used.** The methods are on no list for agents or other stations, and are never forwarded between stations in either direction, so a password never travels from one station to another.
- **The password** is asked for only after a first try without one fails (Tailscale SSH or an SSH key that already works needs none). It is used for one connection and held in memory until ssh has asked for it once, or the connection ends. It is never written to a file, a log, the ship's log, an event, an error or a command line or environment: ssh gets it by running spandeck itself as its askpass helper, which is handed it once over a private socket, and only for a prompt that asks for a password. A second prompt gets nothing, so a wrong password is tried once. The ship's log records who added which machine as which user and how it went.
- **Host keys.** The address is the one the local Tailscale client gives for the machine picked, and must be a Tailscale address; a caller names a machine, never an address. The first connection saves the machine's host key in `known_hosts` in the state directory (ssh's accept-new); a different key later is an error, shown plainly, and nothing is sent. The owner's own ssh config is not read, so it cannot redirect the connection or switch checking off.
- What the machine prints is shown as it runs. It is text from somewhere else: control characters are dropped and the Control Deck inserts it as text.
- `add_machine_cmd` replaces the ssh command (`{user}`, `{host}`, `{known_hosts}`); the script arrives on its standard input. The script uses only commands every release has, since the release it installs may be older than the station that sends it.

### What the owner hit adding a Mac from an iPhone (17 Nov 2026, the owner's report)

Adding the Mac "monitoring" from the deck served by spark2, on an iPhone: "I don't see a place to enter a password, should this pop up a username and password dialog? What does turn off spark2 mean? What if spark2 is down?" "I can't even see what I am typing for username." After Connect and install the dialog said, in the warning colour, "spandeck is running on monitoring but could not ask to join this fleet: on that machine run `spandeck fleet --join spark2`", over a log that ended "spandeck is running. / spandeck: no station called spark2 was found".
